乐曲	yu è qǔ	a musical composition; a piece of music — 乐 yuè (music) + 曲 qǔ (tune; melody). A composed piece of music: 一首乐曲 "a piece of music." The 曲 names the melody, the 乐 the musical art it belongs to.
乐趣	lè qù	delight; pleasure; the fun of a thing — 乐 lè (joy) + 趣 qù (interest; fun). The pleasure or enjoyment found in an activity: 读书的乐趣 "the pleasure of reading."
乐山乐水	yào shān yào shuǐ	to take joy in mountains and water; to be a person of refined nature — A condensed echo of the Analects line. Note the alternative reading: in this fixed phrase 乐 is sometimes read yào in the classical pronunciation (the same character as 乐 lè / yuè, with multiple historical readings , yào here marks affective preference).
乐意	lè yì	to be glad to; willing — 乐 lè (glad) + 意 yì (intention; will). To be happy to do something, with the nuance of genuine willingness: 我很乐意帮忙 "I'd be glad to help."
了结	liǎo jié	to conclude; to settle; to bring to a definitive end — 了 liǎo (to conclude) + 结 jié (knot; tied up; settled). To tie off and close a matter definitively. Used for settling disputes, concluding legal cases, or resolving something that has dragged on without resolution. Has a formal or literary flavor , matters of weight are 了结d.
离 + 时间点 + 还有	lí + ti me + hái yǒu	until (a point in time) — 离 measures distance in time as well as space. Naming a future point, then 还有 ("there still is") plus a span, counts down to it: 离新年还有两个星期 ("there are still two weeks until New Year"), 离上课还有五分钟 ("five minutes until class"). It is the natural way to say "how long until" something.
离别	lí bié	to part, to take leave (of people) — 离 (to part) plus 别 (bié, to part, to bid farewell). A literary word for the parting of people, weighted with feeling. It carries the ache of long goodbyes.
离婚	lí hūn	to divorce — 离 (to part) plus 婚 (hūn, marriage). A separable verb: the two halves can split apart, mirroring the meaning. 离了婚 (líle hūn) means got divorced.
离开	lí kāi	to leave, to depart from — 离 (to part) plus 开 (kāi, to open, to move away). The most common verb for leaving a place or a person. It takes a direct object: you leave something.
离去	lí qù	to leave; to depart (with finality) — 离 lí (to separate from; to leave; distance) + 去 qù (to go away). To separate and go , departure with a sense of severance. More emphatic and often more final than 走 alone. Used for leaving a job, a relationship, a city, or a life. 她悄悄地离去了 (She quietly departed) , the 去 carries the irreversibility.
离职	lí zhí	to resign, to leave a post — 离 (to leave) plus 职 (zhí, post, office). The formal verb for quitting a job or stepping down from a position.
黎明	lí míng	dawn; daybreak
李自成	Lǐ Zì chéng	rebel leader who took Beijing in 1644
理发	lǐ fà	to get a haircut; to have one's hair done — 理 lǐ (to tidy; put in order) + 发 fà (hair). To have the hair cut or styled: 理发店 "barbershop, hair salon." A separable verb, so 理了发 "got a haircut."
理论	lǐ lùn	theory; a body of principles — 理 lǐ (principle, pattern, reason) + 论. A theory: an organised set of principles, as opposed to practice (实践 shíjiàn). 理论上 lǐlùn shàng "in theory" pairs with 实际上 shíjì shàng "in practice" in the standard contrast.
理想	lǐ xiǎng	ideal; aspiration; ideal (adjective) — 理 lǐ (principle; pattern; the inherent structure of things) + 想 xiǎng (thought; thinking). Principled thinking: the vision of how things ought to be, arrived at by sustained reflection on value and order. Functions as a noun (an ideal, a dream) and as an adjective (ideal conditions, ideal outcome).
理学	lǐ xu é	School of Principle; Neo-Confucianism in the Zhu Xi tradition — The dominant Neo-Confucian tradition associated with Zhu Xi and the Song dynasty. Holds that 理 (moral principle) is immanent in external things and texts, and must be reached through gradual investigation and learning.
理由	lǐ yóu	a reason; grounds; justification — 理 lǐ (principle) + 由 yóu (cause; from). The specific reason or grounds given for an action or claim: 没有理由 "there is no reason," 充分的理由 "ample justification."
力量	lì liàng	strength: force, power, capability — 力 lì (force, strength) + 量 liàng (measure, amount). The measurable quantity of force, whether physical, social, or moral: 体力的力量 (bodily strength), 团结的力量 (the power of unity), 知识就是力量 (knowledge is power).
立春	lì chūn	Start of Spring (solar term) — 立 (lì, to stand/establish) + 春 (spring). The first of the twenty-four solar terms (节气 jiéqì), falling around February 4, when the year's farming cycle is reckoned to begin. 立 marks a season being set up, the same verb in 立夏, 立秋, 立冬.
利己	lì jǐ	to benefit oneself; self-interested — 利 lì (benefit) + 己 jǐ (self). Acting for one's own advantage. Often paired in contrast: 利己 vs. 利人 (to benefit others), and condemned in the phrase 损人利己, "harm others to profit oneself."
利用	lì yòng	to make use of; to leverage; to exploit — 利 lì (benefit; advantage; profit) + 用 yòng (to use). To use something for advantage, to derive profit from a resource, opportunity, or person. Context determines valence: 利用时间 (make good use of time) is positive; 利用别人 (exploit others) is negative.
例如	lì rú	for example; for instance; such as — 例 lì (example; case) + 如 rú (like). The standard phrase for introducing an instance: 例如这本书 "for example, this book." Its close cousin is 比如 bǐrú, which is more colloquial.
联合	lián hé	to unite; to join together; joint; united — 联 lián (to link; to connect) + 合 hé (to join). To link separate parties into a single front: 联合国 "the United Nations," 联合声明 "a joint statement." The uniting is formal and often institutional.
脸蛋	liǎn dàn	cheeks; (affectionate) little face — 脸 (face) + 蛋 (egg, oval). The egg-smooth oval of the cheeks, a warm word usually aimed at children or a pretty face. Often softened further to 脸蛋儿 (liǎndànr) with the northern -r ending.
脸红	liǎn hóng	to blush
脸面	liǎn miàn	face (combining both concepts); dignity; decency — A compound that fuses both face concepts. 脸面 covers the full spectrum , both the social prestige dimension of 面子 and the moral character dimension of 脸. Often used in contexts where both are at stake: 要顾脸面 (to consider one's dignity) means both social standing and personal integrity.
脸盆	liǎn pén	washbasin — 脸 (face) + 盆 (basin). The basin you fill to wash your face, a fixture of older Chinese households before plumbing reached every sink. A small window onto domestic life packed into two characters.
脸皮	liǎn pí	facial skin; (figurative) sense of shame; nerve — 脸 (face) + 皮 (skin). The skin of the face, and by extension one's capacity for shame. 脸皮厚 (liǎnpí hòu), thick-skinned, means shameless or unembarrassable; 脸皮薄 (liǎnpí báo), thin-skinned, means easily mortified.
脸谱	liǎn pǔ	painted faces
脸色	liǎn sè	complexion; facial expression; mood read off the face — 脸 (face) + 色 (color, look). Literally the color of the face, used both for physical complexion and for the mood it betrays. To 看脸色 (kàn liǎnsè) is to read someone's expression and act accordingly, a core social skill.
练兵	liàn bīng	to drill troops
练习	liàn xí	to practice; an exercise — 练 (drill) + 习 (to study, to be habituated). The two near-synonyms stack into the all-purpose word for practicing, and as a noun for a practice exercise or drill set.
练字	liàn zì	to practice writing characters; to practice calligraphy — 练 liàn (to practice; to train; to drill; originally, to boil silk to soften it) + 字 zì. Implies regularity, discipline, and sustained improvement. 练字 is deliberate, structured practice, not casual doodling.
良心话	liáng xīn huà	honest words; words from the conscience; what one genuinely means — Speech that comes directly from the conscience, stripped of social performance, flattery, or evasion. The preface 说句良心话 signals: what follows is my genuine view, even if it is unwelcome.
良知	liáng zhī	innate moral knowledge; knowing good and evil without deliberation — The philosophical term from Mencius, developed by Wang Yangming into the center of his system. More formal and theoretical than 良心; primarily found in philosophical and intellectual contexts.
两岸	liǎng àn	Both shores — Both shores: the standard PRC and Taiwan term for the cross-strait relationship: literally "both banks" (of the Taiwan Strait). 两岸关系 (cross-strait relations) is the neutral term used by both governments.
两边	liǎng biān	both sides; the two sides — 两 liǎng + 边 biān (side). Both sides, the two sides of something, drawing directly on 两's sense of a balanced pair: 马路两边 (both sides of the road), 两边都同意 (both sides agree). Related are 两手 (both hands), 两旁 (on either side), and 两端 (the two ends).
两点	liǎng diǎn	two o'clock; two points — 两 liǎng + 点 diǎn (o'clock; point). Two o'clock: 下午两点 (two in the afternoon). Because 点 functions as a measure word here, the two must be 两, not 二. The same logic gives 两点意见 (two points of opinion).
两个	liǎng gè	two (of something); the two — 两 liǎng + 个 gè (the general measure word). The basic, high-frequency way to count two of almost anything: 两个人 (two people), 两个问题 (two questions), 两个小时 (two hours). The use of 两 rather than 二 here is obligatory, since a measure word follows.
量体温	liáng tǐ wēn	to take a temperature: to measure body heat — 量 liáng (to measure) + 体温 tǐwēn (body temperature). A high-frequency everyday verb-object phrase: to take someone's temperature with a thermometer. The same 量 measures other body data in parallel phrases, 量血压 (liáng xuèyā, to take blood pressure), 量身高 (liáng shēngāo, to measure height).
量子	liàng zi	Quantum — Quantum. 量子力学 is quantum mechanics; 量子纠缠 (entanglement, "quantum entwining") supplies metaphors for codependent relationships in internet slang.
潦草	liáo cǎo	sloppy (writing)
猎狗	liè gǒu	hunting dog, hound — 猎 (liè, to hunt) + 狗. A dog bred or trained to hunt: pointer, retriever, hound. The character 猎 itself carries the beast radical 犭, so the compound stacks two animal-marked graphs.
灵活	líng huó	flexible; nimble; agile; adaptable — 灵 líng (quick, clever, responsive) + 活 huó (lively, free). Able to move or change freely: nimble fingers, a flexible schedule, a supple mind. The opposite is 死板 sǐbǎn (rigid, literally dead-stiff), where the water has stopped flowing.
零件	líng jiàn	spare part; component part — 零 líng (fragmentary; odd; loose) + 件 jiàn (a piece). The small individual parts that make up a machine, especially replaceable ones. 零 carries the sense of scattered, loose pieces, so 零件 is the loose hardware: screws, gears, the bits kept in a parts drawer.
零钱	líng qián	small change; loose coins — 零 líng (zero; scattered; odd bits) + 钱 qián. Change, small bills and coins. Increasingly rare in mainland China where mobile payment (微信支付/支付宝) dominates. 找钱 zhǎo qián = to give change. 整钱 zhěng qián = a whole bill (no change needed).
零食	líng shí	snacks; between-meal food — 零 líng (small, piecemeal) + 食 shí (food). The bits eaten between whole meals: snacks, nibbles, treats. 吃零食 is to snack. The word reflects 零's sense of the scattered and fractional, food taken in small odd amounts rather than as a proper sit-down meal (整餐).
零下	líng xià	below zero; sub-zero (temperature) — 零 líng (zero) + 下 xià (below). Below zero degrees, the standard way to state freezing temperatures: 零下十度 (minus ten degrees). Its counterpart 零上 (above zero) is less often said, since positive temperatures are usually given as a bare number.
六法	liù fǎ	Six Principles
六合	liù hé	the six directions; the whole universe — 六 liù + 合 hé (to unite; union). The four compass directions plus up and down, hence the three-dimensional totality of space, the entire cosmos.
六祖坛经	Liù zǔ tán jīng	the Platform Sutra of the Sixth Patriarch · ~20,000 characters · Tang dynasty — The only Chinese-authored Buddhist text designated a sutra. The recorded teachings of Huineng (惠能, 638-713 CE), the sixth patriarch of Chan and the founder of the Southern School.
录音	lù yīn	to record; a recording
路程	lù chéng	journey distance, road length
路灯	lù dēng	street lamp — 路 (lù, road) + 灯 (lamp): the tall lights lining a street. They come on at dusk and mark the shape of the road after dark.
绿灯	lǜ dēng	green light
绿叶红边	lǜ yè hóng biān	green leaf red edge — the visual sign of oolong oxidation
轮船	lún chuán	steamship, large powered ship — 轮 (wheel) + 船 (boat): the wheel is the paddle wheel of the first steamers, so the word came to mean any large engine-driven ship. Today it is the everyday term for a big passenger or cargo liner.
论文	lùn wén	a paper; a thesis; a dissertation — 论 + 文 wén (writing, text). A formal academic argument in writing: a journal paper, a term paper, a graduate thesis. 毕业论文 bìyè lùnwén "graduation thesis," 发表论文 fābiǎo lùnwén "to publish a paper."
马可·波罗	Mǎ kě Bō luó	Marco Polo
马力	mǎ lì	horsepower — 马 (horse) + 力 (power, force). A loan-translation of "horsepower," the unit that measures an engine in horses, keeping the animal alive inside the machine that replaced it.
马路	mǎ lù	street, paved road, main thoroughfare — 马 mǎ (horse) + 路 lù (road). Originally "horse road," wide and packed enough to take horse traffic, in contrast with footpaths. In modern usage 马路 is the standard word for a paved urban street, especially one with vehicle traffic. Crossing it: 过马路 guò mǎlù.
码头	mǎ tou	pier; also: turf (underworld)
馒头	mán tou	steamed bun
满意	mǎn yì	satisfied; pleased; content — 满 mǎn (full) + 意 yì (heart; wish). The heart filled to its wish, hence satisfied: 对结果很满意 "very pleased with the result." Its opposite is 不满 bùmǎn, dissatisfied.
漫画	màn huà	comics; cartoon; manga — 漫 màn (free; unrestrained) + 画 huà (drawing). A free, sketchy drawing: the word for comics and cartoons. The same two characters name Japanese manga, where they are read manga.
毛笔	máo bǐ	writing brush; ink brush — 毛 máo (hair; fur) + 笔 bǐ. The classic soft-tipped brush of calligraphy and ink painting, named for the animal hair of its tuft. The tool that gives Chinese writing its living, varying line.
矛盾论	máo dùn lùn	On Contradiction — On Contradiction: Mao Zedong's 1937 philosophical essay applying materialist dialectics to political analysis. One of the two foundational texts of Mao Zedong Thought (alongside 实践论 Shíjiàn Lùn, "On Practice").
冒出	mào chū	to pop up; to suddenly emerge
贸易公司	mào yì gōng sī	trading company
没问题	méi wèn tí	no problem; sure; that's fine — One of the most useful everyday phrases, used to agree to a request, reassure, or confirm that something will be fine. Equivalent to "no problem / sure / consider it done." A warm, willing reply.
梅雨	méi yǔ	plum rains
美国	Měi guó	USA (lit. "beautiful country") — 美 měi (beautiful) + 国 guó. From the phonetic transcription of "America" (亚美利加 Yà-Měi-lì-jiā), with 美 selected as both sound-match and flattering character. A lucky phonetic fit.
美好	měi hǎo	beautiful; wonderful
美丽	měi lì	beautiful (poetic, elevated) — 美 měi + 丽 lì (splendid; brilliant , originally: a deer with fine antlers). Used for landscapes, natural scenes, and elevated emotional beauty. More literary than 漂亮. 美丽的风景 "beautiful scenery" · 美丽的心灵 "a beautiful soul."
美女	měi nǚ	miss (lit. beauty); address for a young woman
美食	měi shí	delicious food; gourmet cuisine; gastronomy — 美 měi (beautiful; excellent) + 食 shí (food; to eat). Fine food; delicacies. Used everywhere food culture is celebrated , travel blogs, TV shows, restaurant descriptions. 美食家 měishíjiā = gourmet; food connoisseur.
昧良心	mèi liáng xīn	to go against one's conscience; to act in bad faith — 昧 mèi (to obscure; to violate knowingly). 昧良心 is the standard phrase for suppressing what one's conscience perceives and doing wrong anyway , the act Wang Yangming calls covering the sun with clouds.
闷黄	mèn yuáng	smothering / yellowing — the defining yellow tea step
孟姜女	Mèng Jiāng nǚ	Meng Jiangnu (the wall-weeping legend)
梦想	mèng xiǎng	dream; aspiration; to dream of — 梦 mèng (dream; to dream) + 想 xiǎng (thought; aspiration). Dream-thought: a vision held in the heart that exceeds the present reality. Warmer and more personal than 理想 (which has a principled, philosophical register); closer to the English "dream" in the sense of a cherished personal ambition.
迷路	mí lù	to lose one's way, to get lost — 迷 mí (confused, lost) + 路 lù (road). Literally "to be confused about the road." Used for getting lost in a city, in mountains, or on the way to somewhere. The figurative sense (losing one's moral or career direction) usually prefers 迷失方向 míshī fāngxiàng or 走错路 zǒu cuò lù.
迷失方向	mí shī fāng xiàng	to lose one's bearings; to be lost (literally or figuratively) — 迷 (mí, to be confused; to lose oneself) + 失 (shī, to lose) + 方向. A heavier and more poetic version of 失去方向. Used for someone literally lost in a forest or city, but more often for someone in a moral, professional, or personal crisis. Common in advice writing, sermons, and the chorus of pop songs.
迷信	mì xìn	superstition
密封	mì fēng	to seal hermetically; airtight — 密 (mì, tight / dense) plus 封 (to seal): to seal tightly so nothing leaks in or out. The word for airtight packaging, vacuum jars, and sealed envelopes.
面前	miàn qián	in front of; before (someone); in one's presence — 面 miàn (face) + 前 qián (front). The space directly before one's face. Often used figuratively: 在困难面前 "in the face of difficulty," 在事实面前 "before the facts."
面子工程	miàn zi gōng chéng	Face engineering — Face engineering: infrastructure or projects built primarily for prestige rather than practical need. Empty airports in small cities, oversized government buildings, unnecessary highways: these are classic 面子工程.
闽南语	Mǐn nán yǔ	Southern Min , the Hokkien topolect spoken in southern Fujian, Taiwan (as 台語 Táiyǔ or 台灣話 Táiwānhuà), and across Southeast Asian Chinese communities in Malaysia, Singapore, Indonesia, and the Philippines.
名词	míng cí	noun — 名 míng (name) + 词 cí (word). The "name-word," a noun: a word for a person, place, thing, or idea. 桌子, 北京, and 自由 are all 名词.
名句	míng jù	a famous line; a well-known quotation — 名 míng (famous) + 句 jù (line). A celebrated line of verse or prose, so well known that it stands on its own apart from the work it came from. The pairing 千古名句 "a famous line for the ages" praises a line that has lasted.
名声	míng shēng	reputation; standing (can be good or bad) — 名 míng (name) + 声 shēng (sound; voice; what is heard). What others say and hear about you , your name as it sounds in the world. Crucially neutral: 好名声 (good reputation) vs 坏名声 (bad reputation). The modifier does the moral work; 名声 itself simply denotes the fact of being talked about.
名实	míng shí	name and reality — the alignment that 正名 seeks — 名 míng (name) + 实 shí (reality, actuality). The pair names the problem: when 名 and 实 diverge, something false is being presented to the world. 名实相符 (name and reality correspond) is the condition 正名 aims to restore.
明了	míng liǎo	to understand clearly
明亮	míng liàng	bright; luminous; well-lit — 明 míng + 亮 liàng (bright; shining). The compound for physical and figurative luminosity. Used for rooms, eyes, futures, and voices (a 明亮的嗓音 is a clear, bright singing voice).
明年	míng nián	next year — 明 míng + 年 nián (year). The same "bright/coming" sense extended to years. Pattern: 明天 tomorrow · 明年 next year · 明月 the bright moon (classical). The character 明 reaches across all timescales.
明确	míng qu è	clear and definite; explicit; to specify — 明 míng + 确 què (definite; certain; firm). Used for rules, policies, goals, and instructions that leave no room for ambiguity. More formal than 明白. 请明确你的要求 = "Please specify your requirements."
明堂	míng táng	Bright hall — Bright hall: the open space in front of a geomantically ideal site, where qi gathers before the building. The concept appears in both palace architecture and burial site theory.
明天	míng tiān	tomorrow — 明 míng (bright; dawn; the next) + 天 tiān (day). The "bright day," the day the sun will rise again. 明 also means "next" in 明年 míngnián (next year) and 明月 míngyuè (the bright moon, a poetic image for longing). Note: in classical Chinese, 明日 míngrì is the literary form; 明天 is modern spoken Mandarin.
明天会下雨	míng tiān huì xià yǔ	it will rain tomorrow — 会 as future/likelihood marker — 会 has a second major function entirely distinct from learned ability: expressing expectation, likelihood, or prediction about a future event. This 会 does not mean "has learned how to" , rain cannot "learn" to rain. Instead, it signals the speaker's confident expectation that something will occur.
明天就到了	míng tiān jiù dào le	arrives as soon as tomorrow; it'll be here by tomorrow already — 就 can also point toward an imminent future event, expressing that something will happen sooner than might be expected. The speaker's attitude: "it's coming up fast, it'll be here before you know it." This future-pointing 就 often co-occurs with 了 at the sentence end.
明显	míng xiǎn	obvious; evident; marked — 明 míng + 显 xiǎn (to show; visible; eminent). What is bright is also visible to all. Used when something is undeniably apparent: improvement, change, a lie. 明显进步 "marked improvement."
明星	míng xīng	star (celebrity) — 明 (bright) + 星: the bright star, classical Chinese for Venus before it became the word for screen and stage fame. 追星 ("chasing stars") is fandom, and a 粉丝 (fěnsī, fan, a sound-loan that also means glass noodles) does the chasing.
墨经	Mò Jīng	the Mohist Canon — logic and proto-science — The 墨经 consists of four chapters within the 墨子 (经上, 经下, 经说上, 经说下) and represents the most systematic engagement with formal reasoning, optics, mechanics, and geometric principles in pre-Han Chinese thought. The Canon defines terms, categorizes knowledge claims, and argues from evidence.
母亲河	mǔ qīn hé	Mother River , the affectionate designation for the Yellow River, emphasizing its role as the origin of Chinese agricultural civilization. The Yangtze also claims this title in southern China, but the Yellow River carries the deeper historical weight.
母语	mǔ yǔ	mother tongue; first language — 母 mǔ (mother) + 语 yǔ (language). Mother tongue , the language absorbed in childhood, tied to family and home. 母语为汉语的人 = native Chinese speakers. 母语习得 = first language acquisition. 目标语 mùbiāoyǔ = target language (the language being learned). 外语 wàiyǔ = foreign language.
木头	mù tou	wood; timber; (of a person) wooden, dull — 木 mù (wood, tree) + 头 tou (suffix). Wood as a material. Used figuratively of a person who is slow or unresponsive: 木头人 "a blockhead," literally a wooden person.
拿出来	ná chū lái	to take out; to produce; to bring out — 拿 ná (take; hold; grasp) + 出来 chūlái (bring out). To produce something from a container, pocket, bag, or interior , making it visible to the speaker. Also used abstractly: 拿出证据 (produce evidence), 拿出方案 (put forward a plan). The 出来 marks the transition from concealed to revealed.
耐心	nài xīn	patience
南北	nán běi	north-south
南方	nán fāng	the South; southern China — 南 (south) + 方 (direction; region). In Chinese cultural geography, 南方 (the South) and 北方 (the North) mark one of the most significant cultural divides in the country: the Yangtze River (扬子江) and the Qinling-Huai River line are the approximate cultural boundary.
南京	Nán jīng	Nanjing (city) — 南 (south) + 京 (capital). The Southern Capital , the counterpart to 北京 Běijīng (Northern Capital). Nanjing served as the Ming dynasty capital from 1368 to 1421, before Yongle moved the court north.
南人	Nán rén	Southerners — fourth class — Former Southern Song subjects, the last conquered and most numerous. Faced highest taxes, most restricted access to office, forbidden from owning weapons. Approximately 85% of the Yuan's total population.
难题	nán tí	hard problem; knotty issue; difficult question — 难 nán (difficult) + 题 tí (problem). A problem that resists easy solution, whether on paper or in life: 出了一道难题 "posed a tough problem." A close cousin is 习题 xítí, the practice exercises a student works through.
脑力	nǎo lì	mental effort
内部	nèi bù	internal; the interior; inside (of an organization) — 内 nèi (inside) + 部 bù (section). The inner part of a body, machine, or group, often the confidential inside of an organization: 内部消息 "inside information," 内部会议 "an internal meeting." Its pair is 外部 wàibù (external).
内心	nèi xīn	inner heart; deepest feelings; the inner self — 内 nèi (inside; inner) + 心 xīn (heart). The deepest, most private layer of a person's interior life, what they feel before they decide how to present it to the world. Common in introspective prose, therapeutic contexts, and literary characterization. 内心世界 = the inner world.
能工巧匠	néng gōng qiǎo jiàng	skilled craftsmen
能力	néng lì	ability; capability; capacity — 能 néng (be able) + 力 lì (strength). The general word for ability, what a person is capable of doing: 工作能力 "work ability," 有能力 "to be capable."
能量	néng liàng	Energy — Energy. Physics term and self-help term alike: 正能量 (positive energy) is the government-endorsed phrase for uplifting content, a usage physicists did not authorize.
宁可	nìng kě	would rather (at a cost)
努力	nǔ lì	to make an effort; to strive; hardworking — 努 nǔ (to put forth) + 力 lì (effort). To bend one's strength toward a goal, the everyday word for trying hard: 努力工作 "to work hard," 努力学习 "to study diligently." Works as both verb and noun.
女儿	nǚ ér	daughter — 女 nǚ (female) + 儿 ér (child; son , used neutrally here). Daughter. The contrast with 儿子 érzi (son , lit.
女孩	nǚ hái	girl — 女 (female) plus 孩 (child). Like 男孩, it freely takes the extra 子 as 女孩子. It covers girls and, in everyday speech, young women.
女朋友	nǚ péng you	girlfriend
排队	pái duì	to line up, to queue — 排 (to arrange in a row) + 队 (a formed line). The verb every visitor learns first: to stand one behind another and wait your turn.
排练	pái liàn	to rehearse
派出所	pài chū suǒ	local police substation; community police office
跑步	pǎo bù	to jog; to run (as exercise); a run — 跑 + 步 (bù, step). The everyday verb-noun for running as exercise. Used both as an activity (我每天跑步, "I jog every day") and as a noun (一次跑步, "one running session"). The word covers everything from a casual neighborhood jog to marathon training.
跑出来	pǎo chū lái	to run out (toward speaker) — 跑 pǎo (run) + 出来 chūlái (come out). The full directional compound: running out of an enclosed space toward the speaker's position. The 来 anchors orientation , the speaker is outside, watching the emergence happen.
跑了	pǎo le	got away; ran off; (of fish, opportunity) escaped — 跑 + 了 (le, perfective particle). A small but very common construction marking that something or someone has escaped or slipped away. The subject is often something one wanted to keep: a thief, a debtor, a fish, a customer, an opportunity.
跑腿	pǎo tuǐ	to run errands
拼音	pīn yīn	Pinyin; the phonetic spelling of Mandarin — 拼 pīn (to piece together) + 音 yīn (sound). The Romanized spelling that pieces a Mandarin syllable from initial, final, and tone. The scaffolding every learner climbs before the characters take hold.
平安	píng ān	safe and sound; at peace; well — 平 píng (calm) + 安 ān (safe). Safe and well, free of harm or trouble: 一路平安 "have a safe trip," 平安回家 "to get home safely." A staple of blessings and farewells.
平常	píng cháng	ordinary; common; usually, ordinarily — 平 píng (level; plain) + 常 cháng (ordinary). Both an adjective (unremarkable, common) and a time word (in ordinary times, usually): 平常的一天 "an ordinary day," 我平常六点起床 "I usually get up at six."
平地	píng dì	level ground; flat land — 平 píng (level) + 地 dì (ground). Flat, even land, as opposed to slopes or mountains: 一片平地 "a stretch of level ground." The opposite is hilly or broken terrain.
平等	píng děng	equality; equal in status — 平 píng (level) + 等 děng (rank). Equal standing, no rank set above another: 人人平等 "everyone is equal," 男女平等 "equality of men and women." More about status than about fairness in dealings.
平凡	píng fán	commonplace
平房	píng fáng	single-storey house
平衡	píng héng	balance; equilibrium
平静	píng jìng	calm; tranquil
平均	píng jūn	average; on average; to even out — 平 píng (even) + 均 jūn (equal). The arithmetic mean, the level reached when a total is shared out evenly: 平均分 "average score," 平均年龄 "average age," 平均分配 "to distribute evenly."
平面	píng miàn	flat surface; plane; two-dimensional — 平 píng (flat) + 面 miàn (surface; face). A flat surface, and in geometry a plane. Also the modifier in 平面设计 píngmiàn shèjì "graphic design," the flat, two-dimensional kind.
平稳	píng wěn	steady; smooth; stable — 平 píng (level) + 稳 wěn (steady; firm). Moving or sitting on an even keel, without jolts or swings: 平稳着陆 "a smooth landing," 物价平稳 "prices are stable."
评论	píng lùn	to review, to comment; a review, a commentary — 评 píng (to appraise, to judge) + 论. To pass critical judgement on something, or the resulting commentary. A film review, a book review, a comment posted under an article (评论区 pínglùnqū "the comments section") are all 评论.
凭借	píng jiè	rely on, by virtue of
菩萨心肠	pú sà xīn cháng	a bodhisattva's heart; saintly compassion — 心肠 xīncháng = the heart-and-gut, the moral center of a person. 菩萨心肠 describes someone of exceptional compassion and forgiveness , a person who cannot bear to see others suffer and who gives without calculation.
普通	pǔ tōng	ordinary; common; general; average — 普 pǔ (widespread; universal) + 通 (general; running throughout). Widespread and holding everywhere: the quality of being ordinary, the common case rather than the special one. 普通人 (an ordinary person), 普通话 (the common spoken standard, Mandarin).
七情	qī qíng	the seven emotions
七情六欲	qī qíng liù yù	the seven emotions and six desires; the full range of human feeling — 七情 (the seven emotions: joy, anger, sorrow, fear, love, aversion, desire) + 六欲 (the six desires of the senses). Together they name the complete inner life of a human being.
齐桓公与管仲	Qí Huán Gōng yǔ Guǎn Zhòng	Duke Huan of Qi and Guan Zhong — the classical model of ruler and reforming minister — 齐桓公 Qí Huán Gōng (r. 685–643 BCE) became the dominant power of the early Spring and Autumn through the administrative genius of his minister 管仲 Guǎn Zhòng. The partnership became the canonical Chinese example of a ruler who provides authority and a minister who provides competence.
齐全	qí quán	complete (in range); all there; fully stocked — 齐 qí (even; all present) + 全 quán (complete). Said of a set or selection where every needed item is on hand: 设备齐全 "fully equipped," 品种齐全 "a complete range of varieties." Stresses that nothing in the expected range is missing.
企业	qǐ yè	enterprise; firm; company (as an organization) — 企 qǐ (to stand on tiptoe; to aspire) + 业 yè (undertaking). A business organization: 国有企业 "state-owned enterprise," 私营企业 "private enterprise," 企业家 "entrepreneur." The standard term for a firm as a legal and economic body.
起草	qǐ cǎo	to draft (a document); to draw up — 起 qǐ (to begin; to raise) + 草 cǎo (draft). To compose the first version of a formal text, a contract, law, speech, or plan. 起草合同 "to draft a contract."
起订量	qǐ dìng liàng	minimum order quantity (MOQ) — "Order-starting quantity," often per colour or per SKU rather than per order. Ask which. Factories will usually trade a lower MOQ for a higher unit price.
起因	qǐ yīn	the cause; what set something off — 起 qǐ (to rise; to start) + 因 yīn (cause). The cause that started a chain of events, the spark that set a matter in motion: 事情的起因 "the origin of the matter."
恰当	qià dàng	apt; fitting
千里	qiān lǐ	a thousand li; a great distance — 千 qiān + 里 lǐ (the Chinese mile). Literally a thousand li, but in practice any great distance. 千里迢迢 qiānlǐ tiáotiáo describes a long, arduous journey from far away.
千里迢迢	qiān lǐ tiáo tiáo	from a thousand li away; across a great distance — 千里 (a thousand li) + 迢迢 (tiáotiáo, remote, far off , a reduplication conveying long distance and weariness). The compound evokes a journey undertaken across great distance with effort and determination.
千里缘	qiān lǐ yuán	a connection across a thousand miles; long-distance destined bond — 千里 qiānlǐ = a thousand li (the traditional Chinese unit of distance; figuratively: any great distance). The phrase evokes connections that transcend physical separation , the meeting of people from far apart who nonetheless find each other.
千秋	qiān qiū	a thousand autumns; the ages; eternity — 千 qiān + 秋 qiū (autumn, here standing for a year). A thousand years, hence the long span of history and posterity. 千秋万代 (a thousand autumns, ten thousand generations) means for all time, down all future ages. 名垂千秋 is to have one's name endure through the ages.
千万	qiān wàn	ten million; by all means; absolutely (must / must not) — 千 qiān (thousand) + 万 wàn (ten-thousand), literally ten million. But its most common use is adverbial and emphatic, urging or warning with great force: 千万要小心 (you absolutely must be careful), 千万别忘了 (whatever you do, don't forget).
铅笔	qiān bǐ	pencil — 铅 qiān (lead) + 笔 bǐ. The everyday pencil, named for the old idea of a "lead" pencil even though the core is graphite. One of the first writing tools a child learns to name.
前辈	qián bèi	predecessor; senior; elder in one's field — 前 qián (before; preceding) + 辈 bèi (generation; people of the same era). Those of earlier generations who walked the path before you in your field, institution, or family. A term of respect: addressing someone as 前辈 acknowledges their experience and positions you as the newer arrival.
前方	qián fāng	ahead; the forward direction; the front (military) — 前 qián (front) + 方 fāng (direction; area; square). A more formal and often grander term than 前面 , used in military contexts, official broadcasts, and literary or elevated speech. 前方记者 (correspondent at the front) is a journalism term.
前后	qián hòu	front-back; before and after
前进	qián jìn	to advance; to go forward; to march ahead — 前 qián (front; forward) + 进 jìn (to advance). To move ahead in space or in cause: 向前进 "advance forward," 勇往直前 the spirit of pressing on. Its opposite is 后退 hòutuì, to fall back.
前面	qián miàn	in front; ahead; the front side — 前 qián (front) + 面 miàn (face; surface; side). The most common everyday expression for spatial "in front." Works for physical space (前面有个红绿灯 , there is a traffic light ahead) and for sequential position (前面的章节 , the preceding chapter).
前排	qián pái	front row — 前 qián (front) + 排 pái (row; line; rank). The front row of a theater, cinema, classroom, or any seated arrangement. Also used figuratively for first-rank prominence: 前排位置 (front-row position) implies visibility and status in any field.
前任	qián rèn	former occupant of a role; the "ex" (ex-partner, ex-boss) — 前 qián (former; preceding) + 任 rèn (appointment; term of office; to hold a post). The person who formerly held a given role or position.
前天	qián tiān	the day before yesterday — 前 qián (front; before) + 天 tiān (day; sky). The day that stands two steps "in front of" today.
前途	qián tú	future prospects; "front road"
前者	qián zhě	the former
巧合	qiǎo hé	coincidence
亲情	qīn qíng	family affection; the bond of kinship — 亲 qīn (kin; close) + 情 qíng (feeling). The affection that runs between family members, the love of parent and child or among siblings. It completes the trio with 爱情 (love) and 友情 (friendship).
青春	qīng chūn	youth; the prime of life — 青 (qīng, green/blue, the color of new growth) + 春 (spring). "Green spring," the years of youth at their freshest. Used for the whole season of being young, often with a note of how briefly it lasts.
轻声	qīng shēng	neutral tone
轻信	qīng xìn	to believe too easily; credulity
倾向	qīng xiàng	tendency; inclination; to lean toward — 倾 qīng (to lean; to tilt) + 向 xiàng (toward). The way a person or thing leans, a tendency or bias: 政治倾向 "political leanings." As a verb, to be inclined toward: 我倾向于同意 "I'm inclined to agree."
清白	qīng bái	innocence; moral integrity
清楚	qīng chu	clear, distinct — 清 (clear water) + 楚 (distinct). The clarity of things and statements: 说清楚 (say it clearly), 看清楚 (see it distinctly). Pairs with 明白 across the divide of world and mind: the map is 清楚, the reader is 明白.
清淡	qīng dàn	Clear and light , the flavor aesthetic of coastal and delta cuisines, particularly Cantonese. Emphasizes the natural flavors of fresh, high-quality ingredients with minimal masking by heavy seasoning. The direct expression of a geography where fresh seafood and produce are excellent and abundant.
清点	qīng diǎn	to check the count: to take stock
清洁	qīng jié	clean: cleanliness
清静	qīng jìng	quiet, still
清理	qīng lǐ	to clean up: to sort out
清凉	qīng liáng	cool, refreshing
清明	qīng míng	Clear and Bright — Clear and Bright: the solar term around April 4–6, one of the few solar terms that is also a public holiday in China (and Taiwan, Hong Kong). The Tomb-Sweeping Festival (清明节 Qīngmíngjié) falls on or near this term: families visit ancestral graves, sweep them, leave offerings, and burn paper goods.
清明节	Qīng míng jié	Tomb-Sweeping Festival
清明节扫墓	Qīng míng Jié Sǎo mù	Qingming — Tomb Sweeping — 清明节 (Clear and Bright Festival), falling around April 4–6 of the solar calendar, is the annual day of tomb sweeping (扫墓 sǎomù). Families travel to ancestral graves, clear away weeds, pour libations, burn paper offerings, and share a meal at the grave site.
清水	qīng shuǐ	clear water: plain water
清汤	qīng tāng	clear soup; clarified stock
清晰	qīng xī	clear and sharp: well-defined
清新	qīng xīn	fresh: clean and new
清真饮食	qīng zhēn yǐn shí	halal food — the most visible face of Hui culture — 清真 cuisine is a distinct tradition within Chinese cooking, developed by Hui communities over centuries. Core prohibitions: no pork (猪肉 zhūròu), no blood, no alcohol (in strictly observant contexts), and meat must be slaughtered according to Islamic rite (念清真 niàn qīngzhēn).
清蒸鱼	qīng zhēng yú	Steamed Fish
情报	qíng bào	intelligence, information
情歌	qíng gē	love song — 情 (feeling, romance) plus 歌 (song). A love song, one of the most productive 歌 compounds in popular music.
情况	qíng kuàng	situation; circumstances; condition — 情 qíng (circumstance) + 况 kuàng (condition). The standard word for a situation or set of circumstances: 了解情况 "to understand the situation," 紧急情况 "an emergency."
情人	qíng rén	lover; sweetheart
情绪	qíng xù	emotion; emotional state; mood (often the volatile side) — 情 qíng (feeling) + 绪 xù (threads; strands). The tangled threads of feeling, an emotional state that can run high or low: 控制情绪 "to control one's emotions," 闹情绪 "to be in a mood; to sulk."
秋千	qiū qiān	a swing (the playground kind) — 秋 qiū + 千 qiān, written 秋千 or 鞦韆. Here 千 is purely phonetic, lending its sound to a word for a swing that has nothing to do with the number. 荡秋千 dàng qiūqiān is to swing on a swing.
求雨	qiú yǔ	rain calling — interceding with sky spirits — Rain-calling (求雨 qiú yǔ) bridges shamanic and folk-religion traditions: the shaman performs extended trance-dancing, drumming, and offering to the spirits of rain and sky (天神 tiānshén), petitioning them to release precipitation.
球队	qiú duì	sports team (ball game) — 球 (ball) + 队 (team). Any club built around a ball sport: football, basketball, volleyball. Swap the first character for the sport's tool.
趋向	qū xiàng	tendency; trend
趋向补语	qū xiàng bǔ yǔ	directional complement
取决于	qǔ ju é yú	to depend on — The follow-up question to every 应该 and 尽量: find the condition behind the hedge. Once the condition is named, it can be negotiated.
去北京的火车	qù Běi jīng de huǒ chē	the train to Beijing — clause + 的 + noun — 的 can follow entire clauses or verb phrases to mark modification of the following noun. This is Chinese's primary relative clause mechanism , there are no separate relative pronouns. The entire clause precedes 的 which directly precedes the noun being modified.
去声	qù shēng	the departing tone; tone 4
全 · 整	quán zhěng	whole vs. entire-and-unbroken — 全 fuses directly onto nouns to mean "the whole": 全家 (the whole family), 全国 (the whole country), 全世界 (the whole world). 整 zhěng insists the unit is complete and uncut: 整个 (the entire), 整天 (all day long), 整整三年 (three full years).
全部	quán bù	all; the whole; entire — 全 quán (whole) + 部 bù (part; section). The complete set of parts, every one included: 全部作品 "the complete works," 全部完成 "all finished." It points at the totality of a definite group of things.
全都	quán dōu	all; every one; without exception — 全 quán (entirely) + 都 dōu (all). A reinforced "all," placed before the verb to stress that the whole group is included: 答案全都对 "every answer is right." The two totality words stack for emphasis.
全国	quáng uó	the whole country; nationwide — 全 quán (whole) + 国 guó (country). The entire nation as one extent: 全国人民 "the whole nation's people," 全国各地 "all parts of the country." A model for 全 + place: 全省 the whole province, 全市 the whole city.
全家	quán jiā	the whole family; the entire household — 全 quán (whole) + 家 jiā (family; home). The family taken as a complete unit: 全家福 "a whole-family photo," 全家团圆 "the whole family reunited." A warm, everyday use of the whole sense.
全靠	quán kào	to rely entirely on; to owe it all to — 全 quán (entirely) + 靠 kào (to lean on; to depend). To depend wholly on one person or thing, with nothing else to fall back on: 全靠大家的努力 "thanks entirely to everyone's effort."
全面	quán miàn	comprehensive; all-sided
全名	quán míng	Full name , surname + given name together — Full name , surname + given name together. 请问您贵姓？(qǐngwèn nín guì xìng?) is the polite form of "May I ask your surname?" — literally "May I ask your honorable surname?"
全体	quán tǐ	all (of a group of people); the entire body — 全 quán (whole) + 体 tǐ (body). The entire body of people taken together, used of members, staff, or citizens: 全体学生 "all the students," 全体起立 "everybody stand up." Where 全部 counts things, 全体 gathers people.
全新	quán xīn	brand new
权力	quán lì	authority; power
泉眼	quán yǎn	the eye of a spring; the place where spring water emerges — 泉 quán (spring) + 眼. The mouth of a natural spring , the small opening from which water bubbles up. Common in classical Chinese poetry and travel writing.
确定	qu è dìng	to confirm; to be sure; certain — 确 què (sure; firm) + 定 dìng (to settle). To pin a thing down as fixed and beyond doubt, or to be sure of it oneself. 我不确定 "I'm not sure," 确定时间 "to fix the time."
确实	qu è shí	indeed; truly
然而	rán ér	"However" (formal) — "However" (formal). The more literary cousin of 不过, common in written and formal spoken contexts. In casual conversation it can sound bookish; in lectures, presentations, and considered discussions it fits naturally.
让我想想	ràng wǒ xiǎng xiang	"Let me think — "Let me think." Direct, slightly more formal than 那个. Appropriate when a question deserves a real answer and you want the listener to wait while you produce one. The reduplicated 想想 softens it, suggesting brief consideration rather than a long pause.
热情	rè qíng	enthusiasm; warmth; warmly hospitable — 热 rè (hot) + 情 qíng (feeling). Warm feeling, used both as a noun (enthusiasm, passion) and as an adjective for a warm, welcoming person: 他很热情 "he is very warm and friendly."
热心	rè xīn	warm-hearted
人才	rén cái	a talented person; talent; capable people — 人 rén (person) + 才 cái (talent). A person of real ability, and by extension the pool of skilled people an organization or country can draw on: 培养人才 "to cultivate talent," 人才市场 "the job market for skilled workers."
人工	réng ōng	artificial; manual labor
人海	rén hǎi	a sea of people; a vast crowd — 人 rén (person) + 海 hǎi (sea). A crowd so large it reads as an ocean of heads. Famous in the phrase 茫茫人海 "the boundless sea of humanity," the throng in which two people might never meet.
人间蒸发	rén jiān zhēng fā	"to evaporate from the human world" — to vanish; to go completely off the grid — 蒸发 (zhēngfā) = to evaporate; to vaporize. A colorful colloquial expression for someone who has completely disappeared from contact , not answering calls, not posting online, impossible to locate. The humor is cosmological: they have not merely left town, they have vacated 人间 entirely.
人情	rén qíng	human feeling; social obligation; the gift economy of favors — Human feeling, social obligation , the accumulated weight of favors, gifts, and consideration exchanged between people. To "owe someone rénqíng" is to be in their social debt. Gift-giving is one of the primary means of building and repaying 人情. The concept is inseparable from 关系.
人物	rén wù	figure; personage; character (in a story) — 人 rén (person) + 物 wù (thing). A notable person, a "somebody," and also a character in a novel, film, or play: 历史人物 "a historical figure," 主要人物 "the main character."
仍然	réng rán	still; as before; yet remaining — 仍 réng (still, as before) + 然 rán (so). Marks that a state holds unchanged despite what might have altered it: 仍然 here, the situation is still thus. Close to 还是 háishì but more written.
日报	rì bào	daily paper — 日 (rì, day) plus 报: a report issued every day. The suffix that names mastheads, as in 《人民日报》 (Rénmín Rìbào, the People's Daily) and 《广州日报》.
日本	Rì běn	Japan
日常	rì cháng	daily; everyday; routine — 日 rì (day) + 常 cháng (constant; ordinary; everyday). The standard adjective for "everyday, routine, daily life." 日常生活 rìcháng shēnghuó = everyday life. 日常用语 rìcháng yòngyǔ = everyday expressions (a category every Chinese textbook includes). 日常工作 = daily work/routine.
日光灯	rì guāng dēng	fluorescent light — 日光 (rìguāng, sunlight, daylight) + 灯 (lamp): a fluorescent tube, named for the cool daylight-white glow it gives. The long white strip light common in offices and kitchens.
日记	rì jì	diary; journal — 日 rì (day) + 记 jì (record). A day-by-day record of one's own life and thoughts: 写日记 "to keep a diary." The 日 marks the daily rhythm of the writing.
日期	rì qī	date; calendar day
日益	rì yì	increasingly; day by day (formal)
肉眼	ròu yǎn	the naked eye — 肉 (flesh) + 眼 (yǎn, eye). The unaided eye of flesh, as opposed to a telescope or microscope.
如此	rú cǐ	like this; so; in this way — 如 rú (like) + 此 cǐ (this). A literary "like this, so," used both to point back at what was said and to intensify: 如此美丽 "so beautiful." The set phrase 原来如此 means "ah, so that's how it is."
如故	rú gù	as before; unchanged; as it always was — 如 (rú, to be like; as if , a woman and a mouth: to speak as the other speaks, to conform to) + 故 (gù, old; former). As before, unchanged since the past. 一切如故 (everything as before) describes a scene that time has not touched.
如果	rú guǒ	if; in the event that — 如 rú (suppose) + 果 guǒ (outcome). The default "if," opening the condition clause and usually answered by 就 jiù in the result: 如果你来，我就很高兴 "if you come, I'll be glad."
如何	rú hé	how; in what way; what about — 如 rú (according to) + 何 hé (what). The literary "how," asking by what means or in what manner: 如何解决 "how to solve it." The spoken everyday word is 怎么 zěnme; 如何 belongs to writing and formal speech.
如今	rú jīn	nowadays; these days
如同	rú tóng	just like; the same as — 如 rú (like) + 同 tóng (same). A slightly literary way to say one thing is just like another, common in description and writing: 如同白昼 "as bright as day." In speech 好像 hǎoxiàng is the everyday equivalent.
如意	rú yì	as one wishes; satisfactory — 如 rú (according to) + 意 yì (wish; intention). Turning out the way one hoped: 称心如意 "perfectly content." A staple of festive blessings, where 万事如意 wishes that every affair go as desired.
软件	ruǎn jiàn	software — 软 ruǎn (soft) + 件 jiàn (a piece). The intangible programs that run on a computer, paired against 硬件 yìngjiàn (hardware), 硬 yìng meaning hard. The soft and hard contrast is a direct calque of the English pair, with 件 supplying the shared unit-of-a-system sense.
赛跑	sài pǎo	to race; foot race — 赛 (sài, to compete) + 跑. The verb-noun for a competitive run. 百米赛跑 (bǎi mǐ sàipǎo, hundred-meter dash) and 长跑赛 (chángpǎo sài, long-distance race) are the standard sports terms.
三才	sān cái	the Three Powers
三清	Sān qīng	Three Pure Ones — the supreme triad of Daoist theology — The highest tier of the Daoist theological hierarchy, above even the Jade Emperor.
三远	sān yuǎn	three distances; three modes of spatial recession — 三 sān (three) + 远 yuǎn (far; distance). Guo Xi's taxonomy from 林泉高致 (1080s): 高远 gāo yuǎn (high distance -- looking up from base to peak, creates grandeur and vertical drama), 深远 shēn yuǎn (deep distance -- looking through receding layers of mountains and valleys, creates mystery and depth), 平远 píng…
三字经	Sān zì Jīng	the Three Character Classic — 三 sān + 字 zì (character; written word) + 经 jīng (classic; canonical text). A classical primer written in three-character rhyming lines, composed in the Song dynasty and memorized by virtually every literate child in pre-modern China.
散文	sǎn wén	prose; essay
山海关	Shān hǎi guān	Shanhai Pass — eastern terminus of the Ming wall
山河	shān hé	mountains and rivers; the land
山路	shān lù	mountain path
伤心	shāng xīn	heartbroken; deeply sad; grieving — 伤 shāng (wound; injury) + 心 xīn (heart). Lit. "wounded heart." The heart as a physical organ that can be injured. Used for real losses: a pet dying, a relationship ending, a deeply disappointing outcome. Stronger and more pointed than 难过 nánguò (distressed, upset).
商场是个江湖	shāng chǎng shì gè jiāng hú	the marketplace is a rivers-and-lakes world — X是个江湖 as a productive formula — The formula X是个江湖 applies 江湖 logic , its hidden codes, dangers, unwritten rules, and webs of obligation , to any competitive domain.
商队	shāng duì	merchant caravan
商量	shāng liang	to discuss: to talk something over, to consult — 商 shāng (to consult, to confer) + 量 liang (measure, toneless here). To talk a matter over with someone before deciding: to weigh it between two minds.
商量一下	shāng liang yí xià	to talk it over — The deferral. Buys time without saying no, and is the expected answer to any significant new proposal. Name when you will reply so it does not read as a brush-off.
商业街	shāng yè jiē	shopping street
上船	shàng chuán	to board a boat — 上 (to go up onto) + 船 (boat): you step up onto a deck, so boarding is 上, and disembarking is 下船 (xià chuán). The same up/down logic governs 上车 / 下车 for vehicles.
上当	shàng dàng	to be fooled; to be taken in; to fall for a trick — 上 shàng (to go up to) + 当 dàng (the pawnshop). From the image of being shortchanged at the pawnbroker's counter, it now means to be cheated or deceived. 上当受骗 shàngdàng shòupiàn is the common four-character pairing.
上海	Shàng hǎi	Shanghai (city); literally upon the sea — 上 shàng (upon; on) + 海 hǎi (sea). China's largest city and busiest port, at the mouth of the Yangtze. The name reads "on the sea," fitting for a town that grew from fishing village to global harbor.
上海话	Shàng hǎi huà	Shanghainese — Shanghainese: the Wu Chinese topolect spoken in Shanghai, mutually unintelligible with Mandarin. A marker of local identity increasingly in tension with the spread of Putonghua as the dominant urban language for the younger generation.
少量	shǎo liàng	a small amount
深远	shēn yuǎn	profound and far-reaching (of influence or impact) — 深 shēn (deep) + 远 yuǎn (far). The compound stacks vertical depth with horizontal reach. Reserved for influence that is both substantial and long-lasting: 深远影响 shēnyuǎn yǐngxiǎng (profound, far-reaching influence) is a fixed collocation that appears in historical analysis and political commentary.
审美	shěn měi	aesthetics; aesthetic judgment — 审 shěn (to examine; to judge) + 美 měi (beauty). The act of aesthetic discernment , the capacity to evaluate beauty. 审美观 "aesthetic outlook/values" · 审美趣味 "aesthetic taste." Common in design, art, and cultural criticism.
甚至于	shèn zhì yú	going so far as to (emphatic formal)
生词	shēng cí	new word; unfamiliar vocabulary item — 生 shēng (raw; unfamiliar) + 词 cí (word). A word you have not yet learned, the staple of every textbook vocabulary list. Its opposite is 熟词 shúcí, a word already mastered.
生活	shēng huó	life; to live; daily living — 生 shēng (to be born, to grow) + 活 huó (to live). The pairing of two life-words covers both the verb (to live, to make a life) and the noun (life as it is lived day to day). This is the everyday word for "life," distinct from 命 mìng, the life that can be lost.
生日	shēng rì	birthday — the day of birth — 生 shēng (birth; life) + 日 rì (day). The universal word for birthday. 生日快乐！Shēngrì kuàilè! = "Happy birthday!" (lit. "birth-day joyful"). 生日蛋糕 = birthday cake. 过生日 guò shēngrì = "to celebrate a birthday" (lit. "to pass through the birthday").
生物	shēng wù	living thing; organism; biology — 生 shēng (to live) + 物 wù (thing). A living organism, and also the name of the school subject, biology. 微生物 wēishēngwù is a microorganism, a "tiny living thing."
生意	shēng yì	doing business
生于	shēng yú	to be born in; to be born at — 生 shēng (to be born) + 于 yú (at, in). The set formal frame for stating birthplace or birth date, standard in biographies and records: 生于1980年 "born in 1980," 生于江南 "born south of the river."
声明	shēng míng	to declare; to state publicly; a statement — 声 shēng (to sound out) + 明 míng (to make clear). To state something openly and on the record, or the formal statement itself: 发表声明 "to issue a statement."
声母	shēng mǔ	initial consonant (of a syllable) — 声 shēng (sound) + 母 mǔ (mother; base). The consonant that opens a syllable, such as the m in mā. Paired with the 韵母 yùnmǔ, the final that carries the vowel and tone.
声誉	shēng yù	prestige; reputation; standing — 声 shēng (name; repute) + 誉 yù (praise; honor). A more formal, weightier word than 名声, leaning toward esteem and public standing: 国际声誉 "international prestige."
圣经	Shèng jīng	the Bible; holy scripture — 圣 shèng (holy; sage) + 经 jīng (scripture). Literally "the holy classic," the standard Chinese name for the Bible. The same pattern names other scriptures: 古兰经 Gǔlánjīng (the Quran).
失业	shī yè	to be unemployed
诗经	shī jīng	Book of Songs
诗句	shī jù	a line of verse; lines of poetry — 诗 shī (poem) + 句 jù (line). A line or verse of poetry, the unit a poem is built and quoted in: 优美的诗句 "beautiful lines of verse." Often used in the plural sense, the lines of a poem taken together.
施工	shī gōng	To carry out construction — To carry out construction: "execute the work." The word on every fence and detour sign. 施工中 means "under construction," on streets and, borrowed, on websites.
石头	shí tou	stone; rock — 石 shí (stone) + 头 tou (suffix). The everyday word for a stone or rock. The bare 石 survives mostly in compounds and set terms; 石头 is the word you say out loud.
时间词 + 光杆动词	shí jiān cí + ve r b	the plain future: tomorrow I go — A future time word with a bare verb is complete and idiomatic: 我明天去上海 (I'm going to Shanghai tomorrow), 下个月放假 (vacation starts next month). No marker is required at all.
实话	shí huà	the truth; honest words — 实 shí (true) + 话 huà (words; speech). Words that match the facts, plainly told: 说实话 "to tell the truth," also a set phrase "to be honest." Its opposite is 假话 jiǎhuà (a lie).
实话实说	shí huà shí shuō	to speak frankly — Inviting candour removes the face cost of a no. Say early that you prefer bad news now to surprises later; engineers in particular will take you up on it.
实际	shí jì	actual; real; practical; reality — 实 shí (real) + 际 jì (juncture; situation). The actual state of things as opposed to the theoretical: 实际情况 "the actual situation," 实际上 "actually, in fact." Of a person, 很实际 means practical and realistic.
实际上	shí jì shàng	in actual fact; in reality
实践	shí jiàn	practice; to put into practice — 实 shí (actual) + 践 jiàn (to tread; to carry out). The doing that turns an idea into action and tests it against reality: 理论与实践 "theory and practice," 实践证明 "practice has proven."
实力	shí lì	real strength; ability
实习	shí xí	internship; to do practical training
实现	shí xiàn	to realize; to bring about; to achieve — 实 shí (real) + 现 xiàn (to appear; to manifest). To make something real, to turn a plan, goal, or dream into an accomplished fact: 实现目标 "to achieve a goal," 实现梦想 "to make a dream come true."
实验	shí yàn	Experiment — Experiment: to test against reality. 做实验 is to run an experiment; 实验室 is the laboratory; 实验中学 is the "experimental high school" found in nearly every Chinese city.
实用	shí yòng	practical; utilitarian; pragmatic
实在	shí zài	really; honestly; (of a person) down-to-earth — 实 shí (real) + 在 zài (to be present). As an adverb it intensifies: 实在太累了 "I'm really exhausted." As an adjective it describes someone solid and unpretentious: 这个人很实在 "this person is genuine and reliable."
食物	shí wù	food — 食 shí (to eat; food) + 物 wù (thing). Food as a category of substance, the general written term: 健康食物 "healthy food." For "a meal" you would say 饭 fàn, and for cooked dishes 菜 cài.
使用	shǐ yòng	to use; to employ; to make use of — 使 shǐ (to cause; to employ; to dispatch) + 用 yòng (to use). The formal, neutral verb for using a tool, a resource, a method, or a right. Appropriate in written and professional contexts. More deliberate in tone than plain 用: where 用 is the bare act, 使用 implies purposeful, authorized deployment.
市场经济	shì chǎng jīng jì	market economy — what China was moving toward — 市场 (market, literally marketplace) + 经济 (economy). The conceptual opposite of 计划经济 jìhuà jīngjì (planned economy, the command economy the PRC had used since 1949).
事故	shì gù	accident; incident; mishap — 事 (shì, matter, affair, event) + 故 (cause, the reason behind it). An accident or untoward incident: literally an event with a cause, usually an unwanted one. 交通事故 (jiāotōng shìgù) is a traffic accident; 安全事故 (ānquán shìgù) is a safety incident.
事件	shì jiàn	incident; event
事情	shì qing	matter; affair; thing (to be dealt with) — 事 shì + 情 qíng (situation; circumstance). The everyday noun for a matter or affair, anything happening that you talk about or handle. The most common way to say "thing" when you mean a situation rather than an object.
事实	shì shí	fact; the actual state of affairs — 事 shì + 实 shí (real; solid). The matter as it really is, a fact. The phrase 事实上 shìshí shàng means "in fact, actually," used to correct an impression or introduce the truth of a case.
事实上	shì shí shàng	as a matter of fact
事业	shì yè	cause; career; lifework; undertaking — 事 shì (matter; affair) + 业 yè (undertaking). The cause or career one commits to, larger and more aspirational than a job: 事业心 "career ambition," 教育事业 "the cause of education." To 干一番事业 is to accomplish something lasting with one's life.
适当	shì dàng	appropriate; suitable; proper — 适 shì (to suit) + 当 dàng (to fit). Describes a measure, amount, or action that is right for the situation: 适当的时候 (at an appropriate time), 适当休息 (rest as appropriate).
适合	shì hé	to suit; to be suited to; to fit — 适 shì (to be apt) + 合 hé (to fit). A verb taking an object: X 适合 Y means X suits Y. 这本书适合孩子 "this book is suited to children."
适应	shì yìng	to adapt; to get used to; to suit — 适 shì (to suit) + 应 yìng (to respond, to meet). To adjust oneself to new circumstances: 适应环境 (adapt to the environment), 适应新工作 (get used to a new job). The response here is the slow one of fitting in over time.
收到	shōu dào	received, noted — The standard acknowledgement. 好的 (hǎo de) is "okay," 明白 (míngbai) "understood," 收到，谢谢 the polite version. Acknowledging promptly is expected; silence reads as a problem.
手表	shǒu biǎo	wristwatch: a watch worn on the wrist (錶 in traditional script) — 手 shǒu (hand; wrist) + 表 biǎo (here the instrument sense, written 錶 in traditional script). A wristwatch. This is the sense that traditional writing marks with the metal radical (錶), since a watch is a metal device whose face displays a reading. Simplified Chinese writes it 表.
手工	shǒu gōng	handmade; handicraft
手机	shǒu jī	mobile phone — lit. "hand machine" — 手 shǒu (hand) + 机 jī (machine). China's most common word for mobile phone , the machine held in the hand. Completely replaced the earlier 手提电话 and the formal 移动电话 in everyday usage. 玩手机 wán shǒujī = "to play on one's phone" (ubiquitous activity).
手记	shǒu jì	handwritten notes; personal record
手写	shǒu xiě	handwritten; to write by hand
手信	shǒu xìn	A small gift brought back from travel — A small gift brought back from travel: a souvenir or local specialty given to family, friends, or colleagues on return from a trip. The term is particularly common in Cantonese-speaking areas and in Hong Kong. The expectation of bringing 手信 from any notable journey is near-universal.
疏远	shū yuǎn	to grow distant from; to drift apart; estranged — 疏 shū (loose; scattered; infrequent; originally: a channel that has grown shallow) + 远 yuǎn (far). The channel of a relationship silting up, the contact thinning until the other person feels far away. Can be transitive (to push someone away deliberately) or intransitive (to drift apart gradually).
熟练	shú liàn	skilled, proficient, fluent — 熟 (cooked, ripe, familiar) + 练 (drilled). The same 熟 used for cooked food: a skill drilled until it is done, no longer raw. Describes a practiced, effortless command of a task.
属相	shǔ xiàng	Zodiac sign, literally "belonging-image — Zodiac sign, literally "belonging-image." The colloquial word for your zodiac animal, used in everyday speech.
属于	shǔ yú	to belong to; to be part of; to fall under — 属 shǔ (to be subordinate to) + 于 yú (to). States membership or ownership: 这本书属于我 "this book belongs to me," 鲸鱼属于哺乳动物 "whales belong to the mammals."
数词	shù cí	numeral
数量	shù liàng	quantity: the amount or number of something — 数 shù (number) + 量 liàng (amount). The neutral, countable "how many / how much" of a thing. 数量 answers questions of scale and stock: how many units, how large a supply.
双十节	Shuāng shí Jié	Double Ten Festival (Taiwan's National Day)
水墨画	shuǐ mò huà	ink-wash painting
水平	shuǐ píng	level; standard; horizontal; one's degree of proficiency — 水 shuǐ (water) + 平 píng (level; flat; equal). A water surface is perfectly level , the universal measure of flatness and standards. From this physical fact, 水平 became the word for any standard of quality or proficiency.
说不定	shuō bu dìng	maybe; hard to say; possibly — 说 shuō + 不定 (cannot be fixed/determined). Means "cannot be stated with certainty," i.e., maybe, possibly. One of the most natural hedges in spoken Mandarin. More casual and spoken than 也许 yěxǔ or 可能 kěnéng.
说出	shuō chū	to say out; to speak out; to voice (what was held inside) — 说 shuō (speak; say) + 出 (emergence complement). To bring words out from an interior state into speech: what was private, unspoken, or held back becomes expressed.
说到	shuō dào	to mention; to come to (a topic); speaking of — 说 shuō (to speak) + 到 dào (arrive at). Speech that arrives at its topic: to come around to mentioning something, to bring something up. 说到这里 (at this point, speaking of this) is a common transitional phrase in both speech and writing.
说到这个	shuō dào zhè ge	"Speaking of which" or "since you mention it — "Speaking of which" or "since you mention it." Pivots to a related topic that the previous remark suggested. Cleaner than 对了 when the connection is more thematic than associative.
说几句	shuō jǐ jù	to say a few words — 说 shuō (to say) + 几 jǐ (a few) + 句 jù (measure word). To make a few brief remarks, the polite formula a speaker uses before a short toast or comment: 我想说几句 "I would like to say a few words."
说了半天才明白	shuō le bàn tiān cái míng bai	finally understood after talking for ages — After a long period of effort, explanation, or struggle , 才 finally. The effort is encoded in the first clause (说了半天 = talked for half a day, idiom for "a long time"); the 才 marks that only after all that effort did the result occur. This is 才 at its most expressive: hard-won achievement.
说明	shuō míng	to explain; to illustrate; explanation; instructions — 说 shuō + 明 míng (clear, bright). To make something clear through speech. As a noun: 说明书 shuōmíngshū = instruction manual. As a verb: 这说明了… = "this demonstrates/shows that…"
说实话	shuō shí huà	"To tell the truth" or "honestly — "To tell the truth" or "honestly." Signals that what follows is candid, sometimes uncomfortably so. Often used before a confession, an admission, or a frank criticism. The English "honestly" works as a direct translation.
四大发明	sì dà fā míng	the Four Great Inventions
四大美人	sì dà měi rén	the Four Beauties of ancient China
四海	sì hǎi	the four seas; the whole country; the entire world — 四 sì + 海 hǎi (sea). The four seas thought to bound the inhabited world, hence the whole realm, everywhere under heaven. 四海之内皆兄弟 ("within the four seas all men are brothers"), from the Analects, is one of the most quoted lines on universal kinship.
四声	sì shēng	the four tones (of Mandarin) — 四 sì (four) + 声 shēng (tone). The four-way tone system of standard Mandarin: high-level, rising, dipping, and falling. The neutral tone 轻声 is counted separately.
宋词	Sòng cí	Song lyric poetry; the classical 词 of the Song — 宋 Sòng (the Song) + 词 cí (lyric). The lyric-poem form at its height, written to set tune-patterns and meant to be sung. Paired with 唐诗 Táng shī, Tang regulated verse, as one of the high points of classical poetry.
送子观音	Sòng zǐ Guān yīn	child-giving Guanyin — the form prayed to for fertility and safe birth — 送 sòng (to send, to give) + 子 zǐ (child) + 观音. The most commonly petitioned form of Guanyin outside formal temple Buddhism. Depicted holding a child, or seated with children around her, this iconographic type is the primary focus of domestic devotion in households hoping for a child.
虽然	suī rán	although; even though — 虽 suī (although) + 然 rán (so). Opens a concession and almost always pairs with 但是 dànshì or 可是 kěshì in the second clause: 虽然 ... 但是 ... "although ..., yet ...".
虽然但是	suī rán dàn shì	although…but (concessive)
随笔	suí bǐ	casual essay; jottings as you go
随手泡	suí shǒu pào	instant-hot kettle / electric gongfu kettle
随意	suí yì	As you like, freely, casually , the softer alternative to 干杯 that allows the recipient to drink as much or as little as they wish. Using 随意 rather than 干杯 is a show of consideration, often offered to guests who are known to drink little or who have already flagged a limitation.
孙子兵法	Sūn zǐ Bīng fǎ	The Art of War — the foundational Chinese military text — Attributed to 孙武 Sūn Wǔ of the state of Wu (late Spring and Autumn / early Warring States). Thirteen chapters on strategy, deception, terrain, and the nature of victory.
缩短	suō duǎn	to shorten
所以	suǒ yǐ	therefore; so; that is why — 所 (that which) + 以 (the means/reason by which). The most common consequential conjunction in modern Chinese. Follows a cause clause (often introduced by 因为) and introduces the result: 因为下雨，所以我没去 (because it rained, so I did not go).
所有 · 全部	suǒ yǒu quán bù	all of, the entirety of a set — 所有(的) + noun names the complete set up front: 所有的学生都来了 (all the students came). 所有 began as "that which is possessed" (所 + 有, what one has), and the possessive flavor survives in 我所有的钱 (all the money I have).
所知	suǒ zhī	that which is known; what one knows
他结婚了	tā jié hūn le	he got married; he is now a married man — Major life transitions , marriage, graduation, birth, death, career changes , are natural territory for sentence-final 了. The 了 frames the event as a change that affects the current situation: the relevant new fact is that he is now married. It answers an implicit question about the present.
他力	tā lì	other-power (Amitābha's)
他们是在上海见面的	tā men shì zài Shàng hǎi jiàn miàn de	they met in Shanghai (FOCUS: in Shanghai, not elsewhere) — Both speakers know they met , the question is where. The 在上海 sits between 是 and 见面的, specifying the location of the known meeting. Common in stories, biographical accounts, and conversations about how relationships began.
他日	tā rì	another day; some future time
他三十岁才结婚	tā sān shí suì cái jié hūn	he didn't marry until thirty (late, in the speaker's view) — From the speaker's perspective, thirty is late to marry , perhaps later than expected or social norms dictate.
他说得很清楚	tā shuō de hěn qīng chu	he speaks clearly — verb + 得 + degree complement — 得 here follows a verb and introduces a complement describing the degree, manner, or result of that action. Think of it as "to the extent that…" or "in a way that…" The complement can be an adjective, another verb, or a clause. This is the most analytically complex use of 得.
它的	tā de	its (modern spoken)
它们	tā men	they, them (non-human)
她来了两个星期了	tā lái le liǎng gè xīng qī le	she has been here for two weeks (and is still here) — The double-了 construction with a duration complement is one of its most important uses. The first 了 marks the initial arrival (completed action of coming); the second 了 marks the current relevance , she is still here now. Without the second 了, the sentence implies the visit is concluded.
台词	tái cí	an actor lines; stage dialogue; script lines — 台 tái (stage) + 词 cí (words). The lines a performer speaks in a play, film, or show. 背台词 bèi táicí is to learn one lines by heart.
台灯	tái dēng	desk lamp, table lamp — 台 (tái, platform, stand) + 灯 (lamp): a lamp that sits on a desk or table, the small pool of light you read and study by.
台海	tái hǎi	The Taiwan Strait — The Taiwan Strait: the body of water between Taiwan and the Chinese mainland. Used as shorthand for the cross-strait political situation: 台海关系 (cross-strait relations), 台海局势 (Taiwan Strait situation).
抬头	tái tóu	to raise the head; to look up — 抬 tái (to lift) + 头 tóu (head). To lift the head and look up, literally or in the sense of holding one's head high after hardship. Its opposite is 低头 dītóu, to lower the head.
太平	tài píng	great peace — the classical political ideal
太平天国	Tài píng Tiāng uó	Taiping Heavenly Kingdom
太阳	tài yáng	the sun — 太 tài (supreme; ultimate; the greatest) + 阳 yáng (yang force; light). The Supreme Yang , the ultimate source of yang energy in the cosmos. In the 五行 framework, the sun is the source of Fire phase energy. 太阳能 (solar energy) uses exactly this logic.
逃跑	táo pǎo	to flee; to escape; to run away — 逃 (táo, to flee) + 跑. The standard verb for running away from a threat, a captor, or an obligation. Used for fugitives, escaped animals, soldiers fleeing a battlefield, and (figuratively) people who avoid responsibility.
讨论	tǎo lùn	to discuss; to talk over — 讨 tǎo (to seek out, to ask for) + 论. The everyday word for discussing a matter: a group talking something through to reach understanding or a decision. Cooperative by default, two or more people pooling views rather than fighting over them.
讨论会	tǎo lùn huì	a discussion forum; a symposium
套路	tào lù	form; sequence; a fixed pattern (also: routine, formula) — A choreographed sequence of martial techniques practiced solo, encoding combat principles. Also used colloquially for any formulaic routine or pattern: 走套路 "going through the motions" · 那只是套路 "that's just standard formula."
特意	tè yì	specially; on purpose (warm or neutral)
提高	tí gāo	to raise; to improve; to enhance — 提 tí (to lift, to raise; to bring up) + 高 (high). To raise the level of something: 提高效率 (improve efficiency); 提高水平 (raise the standard); 提高工资 (increase wages). The standard verb for improvement in formal and professional contexts.
提前	tí qián	to move up in time; to do in advance; ahead of schedule — 提 tí (to lift; to bring forward; to raise up) + 前 qián (front; before). Literally "to lift forward": to move a scheduled event earlier than planned, to do something in advance. The opposite is 推迟 tuīchí (to postpone, to push back).
题款	tí kuǎn	inscription on a painting
题诗	tí shī	to inscribe a poem (on a painting, wall, or scroll) — 题 tí (to inscribe) + 诗 shī (poem). To write a poem directly onto a surface, a classic gesture in Chinese painting where verse and image share one scroll.
题字	tí zì	to inscribe characters; an inscription — 题 tí (to inscribe) + 字 zì (characters). To brush words onto a painting, plaque, or wall, or the inscription itself. Often done by a respected figure as a mark of honor.
体力	tǐ lì	physical stamina; bodily strength; endurance — 体 tǐ (body) + 力 lì (strength). The body's stamina, the strength that sustains physical effort over time. Often paired against 脑力 nǎolì (mental effort): 体力劳动 "physical labor."
体用	tǐ yòng	substance and function; essence and manifestation — 体 tǐ (body; substance; the underlying essence) + 用 yòng (function; use; the outward expression). A compound used in philosophy, aesthetics, Buddhist thought, and political theory to describe the relationship between a thing's nature and its operation in the world.
替换	tì huàn	to replace, substitute — 替 (in place of) + 换 (swap). To take one thing out and put a stand-in where it was: a worn part, a player, a placeholder word. The replacement fills the same slot the original left.
天才	tiān cái	genius; heaven-bestowed talent — 天 tiān (heaven) + 才 cái (talent; ability). Heaven-bestowed ability , the kind of capacity that seems to have come from beyond ordinary human effort. The etymology encodes a theory of talent: genius is a gift, not a product.
天色	tiān sè	color of the sky; time of day as read from the light — 天 tiān (sky) + 色 sè (color; hue; appearance). The quality of sky-light as a way of telling the time or mood of the day: dawn's pale gray, midday's high white, dusk's orange bleed. A literary and slightly classical usage, common in classical fiction and poetry.
天文	tiān wén	Astronomy — Astronomy: heaven's patterns. 天文数字 ("an astronomical figure") is the standard idiom for an unthinkably large sum, same metaphor as English.
天意	tiān yì	the will of Heaven; what fate decrees
天真	tiān zhēn	naive; innocent; childlike; guileless — 天 tiān (heaven) + 真 zhēn (genuine; true; real). "Heaven-true" , the quality of someone whose genuine nature has not been shaped or distorted by social calculation. In children, this is charming: 天真烂漫 (innocent and carefree , the enchanting naivety of childhood).
铁路	tiě lù	railway
听到	tīng dào	to hear; to catch (a sound) — 听 tīng (to listen) + 到 dào (arrive). Listening that arrived: the sound reached the ear and registered. 听到 is not an attempt to hear but a confirmed result. Compare 听 alone (the action of listening) with 听到 (the listening that succeeded).
听懂	tīng dǒng	to understand what one hears; to follow spoken language — 听 (to listen) + 懂 dǒng (to understand). The resultative complement 懂 marks successful comprehension , listening that reached understanding. Constant in language learning contexts: 你听懂了吗？ (Did you understand what you heard?) contrasts with 你听到了吗？ (Did you hear it? — did the sound reach you?).
听音乐	tīng yīn yu è	to listen to music — 听 (to listen) + 音乐 yīnyuè (music , 音 sound/tone + 乐 joy/music). The most common collocation for 听 in daily life. 听歌 tīng gē (to listen to songs) is the slightly more casual form.
停电	tíng diàn	power outage
通常	tōng cháng	usually; ordinarily; generally; as a rule — 通 (general; holding throughout) + 常 cháng (constant; usual; frequent). The case that holds across most instances: usually, as the general rule. 通常 sets up the ordinary expectation against which exceptions are measured: 我通常七点起床 (I usually get up at seven).
通车	tōng chē	to be open to traffic; to begin service (of a road or line) — 通 (to open; to be through) + 车 chē (vehicle). To be through to vehicles: the moment a new road, bridge, railway, or subway line opens and traffic can run along it. A staple of infrastructure news, where the opening of a line is itself an event: 正式通车 (officially opened to traffic).
通过	tōng guò	to pass through; to get past; by means of; via — 通 (to go through) + 过 guò (to cross; to pass). The everyday verb for getting through something: passing through a gate, passing an exam, a motion passing a vote.
通信	tōng xìn	to correspond; to communicate; telecommunications — 通 (to connect; to convey) + 信 xìn (a letter; a message; trust). To get messages through to one another. Historically it meant to exchange letters: 我们通了多年信 (we corresponded for many years).
通用	tōng yòng	general-purpose; in common use; interchangeable — 通 (general; running throughout) + 用 yòng (to use). Usable throughout, across cases: not specialized to one situation but serving generally. 通用 describes something accepted everywhere or applicable across the board: 全国通用 (valid nationwide), 通用规则 (a general rule that applies in all cases).
通知	tōng zhī	to notify; to inform; a notice; a notification — 通 (to get through; to convey) + 知 zhī (to know; to make known). To make something known by getting word through: the standard word for an official notification and the act of giving it. Works as a verb (请通知大家, please notify everyone) and a noun (一份通知, a notice).
同意	tóng yì	to agree; to consent; to approve — 同 tóng (same) + 意 yì (idea; intention). To hold the same idea, to give one's assent: 我同意你的看法 "I agree with your view." The opposite is 反对 fǎnduì, to oppose.
痛快	tòng kuài	frank and direct; thoroughly enjoyable — 痛 tòng (pain; with full intensity) + 快 kuài (free-flowing; pleasurable). Two distinct but related uses. As a personality or speech descriptor: so direct and honest it cuts , blunt, refreshingly frank. As an experience descriptor: so wholly satisfying that nothing was held back.
头发	tóu fa	hair (on the head) — 头 tóu (head) + 发 fà (hair). The hair on a person's head, as a mass. Note that 发 here is the fà / 髮 reading; in casual speech the syllable often softens toward neutral tone. Distinct from 毛 máo, the word for body hair and fur.
头七	tóu qī	the first seven-day memorial after death — 头 tóu (head; first) + 七 qī (seven). Traditional Chinese mourning involves rituals at seven-day intervals for 49 days , the period the soul is believed to linger. 头七 (day 7) is the most important: the family burns paper offerings and sets a place for the deceased at the table.
头疼，不能去	tóu téng , bù néng qù	headache, can't go — circumstance prevents ability — 能 covers physical capacity (your body can or cannot do something) and circumstantial ability (the situation allows or does not allow).
突然	tū rán	sudden; abrupt; suddenly — 突 tū (to dash out, abrupt) + 然 rán (-ly). Unlike 忽然, 突然 can also be a full adjective: 很突然 "very sudden," 突然的变化 "an abrupt change." So it covers both "suddenly" and "abrupt."
团队	tuán duì	team (working unit) — 团 (a tight cluster, a ball of people) + 队 (organized group). The modern office and startup word for a project team bound by a shared task.
团结	tuán jié	to unite; unity; solidarity — 团 tuán (round; group) + 结 jié (to bind). To draw a group tightly together into one body: 团结一致 "united as one." Used of teams, communities, and nations holding together against difficulty.
推手	tuī shǒu	pushing hands — the partner practice of Tai Chi
脱离	tuō lí	to break away from, to detach from — 脱 (tuō, to shed, to slip off) plus 离 (to part). To break free of a tie, a danger, or a group. Stronger than 离开: it implies cutting a connection, not just walking out.
驼队	tuó duì	camel train
外表	wài biǎo	outward appearance: the way someone or something looks from outside — 外 wài (outer; outside) + 表 biǎo (surface). The outward appearance of a person or thing, especially how they look to others. Where 表面 leans toward physical surfaces and abstract superficiality, 外表 is most natural for the looks of people and objects, the impression they give at first sight.
外部	wài bù	external; the outside
外发	wài fā	to send out (to a subcontractor) — The question the tour exists to answer. A factory that sends out a step is not necessarily hiding anything, but you need to know, because quality on that step is out of its direct control.
外语	wài yǔ	foreign language — 外 wài (foreign) + 语 yǔ (language). A language other than one''s own. 学外语 "to study a foreign language," 外语学院 "a foreign-languages institute."
完成	wán chéng	to complete; to accomplish; to finish — 完 wán (whole; to finish) + 成 chéng (to bring to completion). To carry a task all the way to done: 完成任务 "to complete a task," 完成作业 "to finish the homework." The everyday word for getting something fully finished.
完好	wán hǎo	intact; in good condition
完美	wán měi	perfect; flawless
完全	wán quán	completely; entirely; fully — 完 wán (finished; whole) + 全 quán (complete). The standard adverb for "completely," to the full degree with nothing left over: 完全同意 "completely agree," 完全没问题 "no problem at all." Negated, it gives "not at all": 完全不对.
完全同意	wán quán tóng yì	"Completely agree — "Completely agree." Strong endorsement. Used when you want to back the speaker without reservation. The 完全 ("completely") makes it emphatic; reserve for moments when full agreement is what you actually mean.
完整	wán zhěng	complete; whole; intact
万事如意	wàn shì rú yì	may all ten thousand things go as you wish; all the best — 万 (myriad) + 事 shì (matters, things) + 如意 rúyì (as one wishes). The standard all-occasion blessing, appearing on red envelopes, greeting cards, New Year couplets, and toasts.
