万物	wàn wù	all things; the myriad phenomena; everything that exists — 万 (myriad) + 物 wù (thing, object, phenomenon). The standard classical and literary expression for "all of existence." In Daoist thought, the ten-thousand things emerge from the interplay of yin and yang, and the Dao underlies them all.
网红	wǎng hóng	internet celebrity
违法	wéi fǎ	to break the law; to act illegally — 违 wéi (to violate; to go against) + 法 fǎ (law). To step over the line the law draws: 违法行为 "an unlawful act." The mirror image of 合法, the foot landing outside the level mark.
为此	wèi cǐ	for this reason; because of this; to this end — 为 wèi (for; on account of) + 此 cǐ (this; the aforementioned). A formal connective linking back to a preceding reason or context and introducing what was done in response. Stronger in register than 因此 yīncǐ (therefore), carrying a sense of specific, deliberate response to the stated situation.
为了	wèi le	in order to; for the sake of; for the purpose of — 为 wèi (for; toward) + 了 le (grammatical completion particle; here a suffix to 为). The standard purpose marker in modern Chinese. 为了 introduces the goal or beneficiary of an action and can appear at the start of a sentence or clause.
为人所知	wèi rén suǒ zhī	to be known by people; to become widely known
为所欲为	wèi suǒ yù wéi	to do whatever one pleases; to act without restraint
位于	wèi yú	to be located at; to be situated in — 位 wèi (position) + 于 yú (at). The standard verb for stating where a place sits, common in geography and description: 北京位于中国北部 "Beijing is located in the north of China."
温饱	wēn bǎo	warmth and food; subsistence — 温 (wēn, warm) + 饱 (fed): warm enough and fed enough, the baseline of a decent life. 温饱 names the threshold of basic livelihood in economic and political language, as in 解决温饱问题 (jiějué wēnbǎo wèntí, to solve the subsistence problem).
文笔	wén bǐ	writing style; literary skill — 文 wén (writing; letters) + 笔 bǐ (pen). The quality of someone's prose, the line their pen draws across a page: 文笔流畅 "fluent, flowing style." Praise or critique aimed at how a thing is written, not what it says.
文房	wén fáng	The scholar study, the room where the Four Treasures are kept and used. Literally "the room of writing." The aesthetic of the study, quiet, ordered, with good natural light, was itself a moral and aesthetic statement about the conditions necessary for serious intellectual and artistic work.
文件	wén jiàn	document; file; paperwork — 文 wén (writing; text) + 件 jiàn (a piece). A written document, an official paper, or a computer file. The word covers both physical paperwork and digital files: on a screen, 文件 is exactly the word for a file, and 文件夹 wénjiànjiā is a folder.
文人	wén rén	literati — the scholarly class who cultivated the guqin — 文 wén (culture; writing; civil) + 人 rén (person). The educated class in imperial China: scholars, poets, painters, officials who defined their identity through cultural cultivation rather than martial or commercial pursuits. The guqin was, more than any other object, the emblem of 文人 identity.
文人画	wén rén huà	literati painting; scholar painting — 文人 wénrén (literatus; cultivated scholar-official) + 画 huà (painting). The tradition of painting as personal cultivation and self-expression rather than as commissioned craft. Theorized by Su Shi 苏轼 and Mi Fu 米芾 in the Northern Song, dominant from the Yuan onward.
文殊	Wén shū	Mañjuśrī — bodhisattva of wisdom — Sanskrit Mañjuśrī ("Gentle Glory"). The bodhisattva of wisdom 智慧 zhìhuì, depicted holding a flaming sword (to cut through delusion) and riding a blue lion. Associated with Mount Wutai 五台山 in Shanxi, China's sacred mountain for wisdom, where Mañjuśrī was believed to reside and manifest.
文学	wén xu é	literature — 文 wén (writing; culture; pattern) + 学 xué (learning). The discipline of literature , both the creative output (novels, poetry, essays) and the scholarly study of it. 文学家 wénxuéjiā = writer, literary figure. 中国文学 = Chinese literature. 古典文学 = classical literature. 现代文学 = modern literature.
文章	wén zhāng	essay; article
文字	wén zì	written language; script; characters collectively — 文 wén (writing; culture; pattern; civilization) + 字 zì (character). Broader than 字 alone. 文字 covers the entire written tradition: individual characters, the system, the culture, and the civilization that writing makes possible.
稳定	wěn dìng	stable; steady; to stabilize — 稳 wěn (steady; firm) + 定 dìng (settled). Holding level without swinging, said of prices, jobs, moods, signals, and states. 稳定的工作 "a stable job," 局势稳定 "the situation is stable."
我不太同意	wǒ bù tài tóng yì	"I don't quite agree — "I don't quite agree." The 不太 ("not too") softens a disagreement into something closer to "I'm not sure about that." Polite, leaves room for further discussion. Common in considered conversations where you want to push back without confrontation.
我明白了	wǒ míng bai le	I understand now; I get it (I have entered a state of understanding) — State-change verbs like 明白 (understand), 知道 (know), 懂 (grasp), 累 (tired), 饿 (hungry), 高兴 (happy) pair powerfully with sentence-final 了. The 了 marks the entry into that state , the moment the change occurs. Before: didn't understand. After: understands.
我是在北京出生的	wǒ shì zài Běi jīng chū shēng de	I was born in Beijing (FOCUS: in Beijing, not elsewhere) — Place focus with 在 + location between 是 and the verb. The presupposition is that the speaker was born , the focus is the location. This is how Chinese speakers typically answer "Where were you born?" — using 是…的 to frame the location as the focused new information.
我要红的	wǒ yào hóng de	I want the red one — 的 stands alone as nominal — When the noun after 的 is already understood from context, it can be dropped , leaving 的 to function as a standalone noun phrase meaning "the one(s) that are X." This is the nominalizing function of 的, productive across all the adjective and clause patterns above.
我一说他就明白了	wǒ yī shuō tā jiù míng bai le	I said it once and he immediately understood — 一 + Verb + 就 is one of the most important patterns in the language: "as soon as / the moment I did X, immediately Y happened." The 就 marks the near-instant consequence , no delay, no difficulty.
我有不同的看法	wǒ yǒu bù tóng de kàn fǎ	"I see it differently" or "I have a different view — "I see it differently" or "I have a different view." The most direct of the gentle disagreement frames. Acknowledges the other view exists and explicitly marks your own as separate. Useful when you actually want to make the disagreement clear.
握手	wò shǒu	to shake hands — 握 wò (to grasp) + 手 shǒu (hand). To shake hands , the standard formal greeting gesture in modern Chinese business and official contexts. Traditional Chinese greeting was bowing or clasping one's own hands together (拱手 gǒngshǒu) rather than hand-to-hand contact.
五经	Wǔ Jīng	the Five Classics — the canonical texts of Confucian education — 五 wǔ (five) + 经 jīng (classic; canonical text; warp thread of a fabric). The five texts that defined literate education from the Han dynasty through the abolition of the imperial examination system in 1905: 诗经 (Classic of Poetry), 书经 (Classic of Documents), 易经 (Classic of Changes), 礼记 (Record of Rit…
五色	wǔ sè	the five colors — green-blue, red, yellow, white, black — 五 wǔ (five) + 色 sè (color, appearance). The five phase-colors: 青 qīng (green-blue, Wood), 红 hóng (red, Fire), 黄 huáng (yellow, Earth), 白 bái (white, Metal), 黑 hēi (black, Water). These are cosmological categories before they are aesthetic ones.
五星红旗	wǔ xīng hóng qí	the Five-Starred Red Flag (flag of the PRC) — 五 wǔ + 星 xīng (star) + 红旗 hóngqí (red flag). The national flag of the People's Republic of China: one large star surrounded by four smaller ones on a red field.
戊己庚辛	wù jǐ gēng xīn	Stems 5–8 (Earth &amp; Metal)
物理	wù lǐ	physics; the principles of matter — 物 wù (thing; matter) + 理 lǐ (principle). Literally "the principles of things," the modern name for physics. A clear case of 理 meaning the lawful pattern running through the physical world.
物体	wù tǐ	object; physical body — 物 wù (thing) + 体 tǐ (body). A physical object regarded as a body occupying space, the term used in physics and plain description alike: 不明飞行物 "an unidentified flying object."
物质	wù zhì	matter; substance; material — 物 wù (thing) + 质 zhì (substance). Matter in the scientific sense, and the material as opposed to the spiritual: 化学物质 "a chemical substance," 物质需求 "material needs."
物质文明	wù zhì wén míng	material civilization
夕阳	xī yáng	the setting sun; evening sun — 夕 xī (dusk; evening) + 阳 yáng (sun). The low sun of late day. By extension 夕阳 marks anything in graceful decline: 夕阳产业 "a sunset industry," the mirror of the 朝阳 sunrise one.
西湖	Xī Hú	West Lake (Hangzhou) — 西 (west) plus 湖 (lake): the storied lake at Hangzhou, ringed by causeways and pagodas, the most painted and most quoted lake in Chinese poetry.
习题	xí tí	practice exercises
洗脸	xǐ liǎn	to wash one's face — 洗 (to wash) + 脸 (face). The first ritual of the morning. A verb-object pair, so you can split it: 洗个脸 (xǐ ge liǎn), to give the face a quick wash.
系主任	xì zhǔ rèn	department head
下雪	xià xu ě	to snow; snow is falling — 下 xià (to come down; to fall) + 雪 xuě (snow). The standard way to say "it is snowing," built on the same 下 used for 下雨 (to rain). Chinese treats weather that falls from the sky as something the sky "sends down," so snow, rain, and frost all take 下.
下雨	xià yǔ	to rain; it's raining — 下 xià (to descend; to fall from above) + 雨 yǔ (rain). Rain descending from the sky. The 下 construction extends to all precipitation: 下雪 (to snow), 下冰雹 (to hail). Notably, 下 is the standard verb for precipitation in Mandarin , not "fall" or "pour" but "descend." The sky issues weather downward.
下雨了	xià yǔ le	it's raining now; it has started to rain — The canonical example of sentence-final 了 announcing a new situation. There is no past tense here , the rain is starting now, and the 了 signals a change of state has occurred or is occurring. This 了 carries the meaning: "now things are different from before."
吓跑	xià pǎo	to scare away; to frighten off — 吓 (xià, to frighten) + 跑. A V+跑 resultative: the action of frightening produces the result of running off. Highly productive pattern: 赶跑 gǎnpǎo (chase off), 撵跑 niǎnpǎo (drive off), 骂跑 màpǎo (scold off). The construction packages cause and escape into one verb.
先知	xiān zhī	prophet (Muhammad)
鲜美	xiān měi	fresh and delicious; savory — 鲜 xiān (fresh; vivid , the "umami" character, fish + sheep 鱼+羊) + 美 měi (beautiful; delicious). The compound for food that is both fresh and deeply flavorful. Note: 鲜 also contains the sheep radical 羊, linking back to 美's own etymology. Used for broths, seafood, and fresh ingredients.
显然	xiǎn rán	obviously; evidently; clearly — 显 xiǎn (visible, to show) + 然 rán (-ly). States that something is plain to see: 显然 he was lying. Often opens a sentence as a comment on the whole: 显然，这是个错误 "clearly, this is a mistake."
险情	xiǎn qíng	dangerous situation
现成	xiàn chéng	ready-made; off the shelf; already prepared — 现 xiàn (on hand) + 成 chéng (completed). Already finished and available, needing no further making: 现成的答案 "a ready-made answer," 现成的衣服 "off-the-rack clothing."
现实	xiàn shí	reality; actual; (of a person) realistic, practical — 现 xiàn (present) + 实 shí (real; solid). What is actually the case here and now, set against 理想 lǐxiǎng (the ideal). As an adjective for a person it can mean clear-eyed, or pointedly materialistic.
线路	xiàn lù	route, line, circuit
相比	xiāng bǐ	in comparison; compared to
相冲	xiāng chōng	Zodiac conflict — Zodiac conflict: signs that are diametrically opposite in the twelve-year cycle and considered incompatible. The Rat-Horse conflict, the Tiger-Monkey conflict, etc.
相处	xiāng chǔ	to get along; to spend time together — 相 xiāng (each other) + 处 chǔ (to dwell; to be with). To live alongside or get along with others: 容易相处 "easy to get along with," 相处了很多年 "spent many years together."
相反	xiāng fǎn	opposite; contrary
相机	xiàng jī	camera
相克	xiāng kè	mutual conquest; the control cycle — 相 xiāng (mutual) + 克 kè (to conquer, to regulate, to check). In the Five Phases context, 克 means disciplined control rather than destruction , the river bank that contains the flood, not the dam that annihilates the water.
相片	xiàng piàn	photograph; photo — 相 xiàng (appearance) + 片 piàn (a flat slice; sheet). The printed photograph as an object. Its everyday synonym 照片 zhàopiàn is equally common; both name the picture you hold or hang.
相亲	xiāng qīn	to meet a prospective marriage partner; a matchmaking meeting — 相 xiàng (to examine) + 亲 qīn (marriage; relative). The arranged meeting where two people look each other over with marriage in mind, often set up by family or a matchmaker. Note: this 相 keeps a level tone for many speakers, but the sense is the inspecting one.
相生	xiāng shēng	mutual generation; the creative cycle — 相 xiāng (mutual, reciprocal) + 生 shēng (to give birth, to generate, to produce). Each phase nourishes and gives rise to the next; each is simultaneously "mother" to the phase it generates and "child" to the phase that generates it.
相同	xiāng tóng	identical; the same — 相 xiāng (mutually) + 同 tóng (same). Two or more things being the same as each other: 完全相同 "exactly the same." More formal and written than the everyday 一样 yīyàng.
相信	xiāng xìn	to believe; to trust — 相 xiāng (mutual) + 信 xìn (trust). To place trust in someone or to believe a thing is so: 我相信你 "I trust you," 相信科学 "to believe in science." One of the most common verbs built on the mutual reading.
相遇	xiāng yù	to meet; to encounter
响应	xiǎng yìng	to respond to; to answer (a call); response — 响 xiǎng (to sound, to echo) + 应 yìng (to respond). To answer a call or appeal, often a collective one: 响应号召 (respond to a call), 积极响应 (respond actively). The image is of an echo answering a sound.
想到	xiǎng dào	to think of; to occur to; to realize — 想 xiǎng (to think; to consider) + 到 dào (arrive). Thinking that arrived at an idea: the thought reached its destination. 想到 implies the idea arrived in mind, often unexpectedly or after searching.
想法	xiǎng fǎ	idea; opinion; what one thinks — 想 xiǎng (to think) + 法 fǎ (method; approach; way). The product of thinking: what thinking produces when it runs long enough. Used freely for ideas, opinions, views, and intentions. 你有什么想法？ is one of the most common ways to ask for someone's opinion in meetings and conversations.
想家	xiǎng jiā	to be homesick; to miss home — 想 xiǎng (to miss) + 家 jiā (home; family; household). The heart facing home from a distance. A verb in Chinese where English uses an adjective ("homesick").
想起	xiǎng qǐ	to recall; to call to mind
想象	xiǎng xiàng	to imagine; imagination — 想 xiǎng (to think) + 象 xiàng (image; likeness; elephant , 象 was borrowed for "image" because of its visual suggestiveness: the elephant is the largest thing you picture). Think-image: to form mental images of things not present. Used as both a verb (to imagine) and a noun (imagination).
想要	xiǎng yào	to want; to desire (explicit) — 想 xiǎng (want; think) + 要 yào (want; need; will). The two wanting words stacked for explicitness or emphasis. Where 想 alone can be tentative (I'd like to; I'm thinking of), 想要 is more direct and pointed. Used when desire is clear and conscious rather than a vague inclination.
向来	xiàng lái	always (up to now); from the start; hitherto — 向 xiàng (facing back along) + 来 lái (coming up to now). A close synonym of 一向, slightly more literary: 他向来诚实 "he has always been honest." Frames a quality as having held from the beginning to the present.
向前	xiàng qián	forward; toward the front; onward — 向 xiàng (toward) + 前 qián (front). Directed at the space ahead: 向前走 "walk forward," 向前看 "look ahead," both literally and in the sense of looking to the future rather than dwelling on the past.
向往	xiàng wǎng	to yearn for; to long for; to look forward to — 向 xiàng (toward) + 往 wǎng (to go toward). The heart facing a place or life it longs for: 向往自由 "to yearn for freedom," 向往美好的生活 "to long for a good life." Warmer and more aspirational than plain "to want."
向阳	xiàng yáng	facing the sun; sunny (of an aspect) — 向 xiàng (toward; facing) + 阳 yáng (sun). Oriented toward the sun, said of a room, window, or slope that gets good light. A 向阳的房间 is a sought-after sunny room.
向右	xiàng yòu	turn right; toward the right — 向 (toward; in the direction of) + 右 (right). The standard directional command for turning right: 向右转 (turn right), 向右走 (go right). As with its mirror pair 向左, used in navigation, military drill, physical education, and any instruction involving directional movement.
向左	xiàng zuǒ	turn left; toward the left — 向 (toward; in the direction of) + 左 (left). The standard instruction for turning or moving left: 向左转 (turn left), 向左走 (go left, walk left). Used in navigation, traffic directions, dance instruction, and any context requiring directional movement.
小聪明	xiǎo cōng ming	cheap cleverness; cunning without wisdom — Small cleverness: the shortcut-finding wit that games the rule and misses the point. 耍小聪明 (deploying petty cleverness) is what teachers accuse corner-cutting students of; its grown-up antonym is 大智慧, great wisdom.
小狗	xiǎo gǒu	puppy, little dog — 小 (xiǎo, small) + 狗. The everyday word for a puppy or any small dog. 小狗 is warm and affectionate, the term a child reaches for first.
小河	xiǎo hé	small river; creek
小女	xiǎo nǚ	my humble daughter (respectful reference) — A father's way of referring to his daughter when speaking to someone of higher status. The 小 does not mean the daughter is young or small , it encodes the father's self-positioning as lesser. Compare 犬子 quǎnzǐ (my unworthy son , lit. "dog son"), the parallel self-deprecating term for sons.
小声	xiǎo shēng	quiet; softly; in a low voice — 小 xiǎo (small) + 声 shēng (voice). A small voice, the opposite of 大声: 小声说 "to speak quietly," often so others do not overhear.
小心	xiǎo xīn	be careful; take care; cautious — 小 xiǎo (small) + 心 xīn (heart; mind). A small heart is one drawn in, watchful, attentive to fine detail. The literal image captures the bodily experience of caution: the heart pulling back, contracting, alert to what is delicate or dangerous.
小心眼	xiǎo xīn yǎn	petty-minded; quick to take offense — Small heart-aperture: a mind with a narrow gate, through which slights enter easily and never leave. The grudge-keeping cousin of 小气, aimed at temperament rather than wallet.
孝心	xiào xīn	filial intention; filial love
笑脸	xiào liǎn	smiling face; a smile worn for others — 笑 (laugh, smile) + 脸 (face). A face shaped into a smile, especially the welcoming one you put on for guests or customers. The opposite expression is 板着脸 (bǎnzhe liǎn), to hold the face stiff and stern.
谐音	xié yīn	Near-homophone, sound-alike , the phonetic mechanism underlying most Chinese number symbolism. 谐音 relationships exist wherever two syllables sound similar enough that one can evoke the other.
写法	xiě fǎ	method of writing a character; stroke order
写实	xiě shí	realism; to depict reality faithfully
写信	xiě xìn	to write a letter
写意	xiě yì	impressionist; expressive — capturing spirit over likeness
写真	xiě zhēn	portrait photograph; lifelike depiction — 写 xiě (to write; to depict) + 真. To depict the real. Originally a classical term for a lifelike portrait painting; in modern usage, a posed portrait photo (especially of a model or celebrity). In Japanese, the same word 写真 shashin simply means "photograph."
心得	xīn dé	insight from experience; what one gained
心动	xīn dòng	heart moved; tempted
心房	xīn fáng	atrium of the heart
心即理	xīn jí lǐ	the mind is principle — Wang Yangming's direct challenge to Zhu Xi. Principle 理 is not located in external things requiring investigation; it is not separate from the moral mind. The mind is itself the site of 理.
心经	Xīn jīng	the Heart Sutra · 260 characters · Xuanzang's 649 CE translation — The shortest and most chanted Buddhist scripture in East Asia. The condensed essence of the Prajñāpāramitā literature: emptiness applied to every Buddhist category in turn, then the closing mantra. Memorized by every Chan novice; recited daily across China, Japan, Korea, and Vietnam.
心里	xīn lǐ	in one's heart; in one's mind; emotionally — 心 (heart, mind) + 里 (interior). Functions as a locative: something is in the heart, occurs in the heart. Covers both cognitive interior (I understand in my mind) and emotional interior (I feel in my heart). More intimate and less clinical than 内心 nèixīn (inner heart , a literary register).
心里话	xīn lǐ huà	sincere words; what one truly means — 心里 (inside the heart) + 话 (speech, words). The words that come from the interior rather than from performance. Sharing 心里话 is an act of trust and intimacy , it signals that the speaker is not managing their presentation but revealing what is actually held inside.
心里有数	xīn lǐ yǒu shù	to have a clear grasp; to know what's what — 心里 (inside the heart) + 有 (to have) + 数 (numbers, a count). To hold the numbers privately in one's interior , to have a clear reckoning of a situation that one carries internally.
心理	xīn lǐ	psychology; mental state; mindset — 心 xīn (heart) + 理 lǐ (logic; principle; grain of wood). "The logic of the heart." Used both as the general term for one's psychological state and as the root of 心理学 (psychology as a discipline).
心灵	xīn líng	soul; inner spirit
心情	xīn qíng	mood; state of mind — 心 xīn (heart) + 情 qíng (emotion; situation). The weather of the inner world. Where 情 alone is broad, covering emotion, circumstance, and romantic feeling, 心情 focuses it: the specific emotional atmosphere a person inhabits right now. It fluctuates by the hour.
心学	xīn xu é	School of Mind; Wang Yangming's Neo-Confucian tradition — The Neo-Confucian tradition associated with Wang Yangming. Holds that 理 is not separate from the mind; the moral faculty 良知 is the ground of both knowledge and action.
心眼	xīn yǎn ( r )	wits; smarts; (also) intent, motive of the heart — 心 xīn (heart) + 眼. The eye of the heart , the inner faculty of judgment and quick-wittedness. 心眼多 xīnyǎn duō "to have many wits" (clever, calculating, or scheming, depending on tone). 心眼好 xīnyǎn hǎo "to have a good heart-eye" (kind-hearted, well-intentioned).
心意	xīn yì	intention; goodwill; the feeling behind a gesture — 心 xīn (heart) + 意 yì (intention; meaning). The feeling or thought behind an action: the sincerity and warmth that a gift, gesture, or visit carries. Often used when downplaying the material value of what you have brought: 这只是一点心意 ("this is just a small token of my regard").
心脏	xīn zàng	the heart (anatomical organ) — 心 xīn + 脏 zàng (internal organ, the biological sense; same character used in 器官, organs of the body). In modern Chinese, 心 alone almost never refers to the biological organ; that role belongs entirely to 心脏. The standalone 心 is always the conceptual, emotional, and cognitive heart.
新华字典	Xīn huá Zì diǎn	the Xinhua Dictionary — 新华 Xīnhuá (New China, the name of the official news agency and many state institutions) + 字典. The most widely-sold book in Chinese history and the standard reference for mainland Chinese students since its first edition in 1953. Simple, portable, and comprehensive for modern literacy needs.
新婚	xīn hūn	newly married; newlywed — 新 (new) + 婚 (hūn, marriage). Newly married , in the fresh state of the new relationship. 新婚快乐 (congratulations on the marriage , Happy Newlywed); 新婚夫妇 (newlywed couple); 新郎 xīnláng (groom , new-man) and 新娘 xīnniáng (bride , new-woman) are the standard terms for a wedding couple.
新疆	Xīn jiāng	Xinjiang
新年	xīn nián	New Year — 新 (new) + 年 (year). The new year , the most basic New Year compound, usable for both the Gregorian New Year (January 1) and the Chinese Lunar New Year. 新年快乐 (Happy New Year) and 新年好 (New Year greetings) are the standard phrases.
新年快乐	xīn nián kuài lè	Happy New Year , literally "new year happy — Happy New Year , literally "new year happy." The standard greeting for the solar New Year (January 1). For Lunar New Year, 新春快乐 (xīnchūn kuàilè) or 恭喜发财 (gōngxǐ fācái, "congratulations and may you prosper") are more appropriate.
新儒家	xīn rú jiā	New Confucianism
新闻	xīn wén	news
新鲜	xīn xiān	fresh; novel; new and interesting — 新 (new) + 鲜 (xiān, fresh , especially of food; vivid; rare). Fresh in the sensory sense: 新鲜蔬菜 (fresh vegetables), 新鲜空气 (fresh air). Also used for novel experiences: 对我来说这是个新鲜事物 (this is something new to me). 鲜 alone means fresh-vivid; 新鲜 combines freshness with newness.
信封	xìn fēng	envelope — 信 (xìn, letter) plus 封 (the sealing/wrapper): the paper sleeve that closes around a letter. The most common everyday word built on 封.
信号	xìn hào	signal; indicator — 信 xìn (trustworthy communication; word) + 号 hào (sign; designation; mark; sound used as a signal). A signal is a word that has been stripped of language and reduced to a reliable mark: it carries only its presence or absence, its strength or weakness, rather than semantic content.
信件	xìn jiàn	letters; correspondence (as a category) — 信 xìn (letter; trust) + 件 jiàn (a piece). A collective, somewhat formal term for letters and written correspondence. A single letter is 一封信 (using 封 fēng); 信件 names the whole category of correspondence, the kind of word a post office or office form would use.
信任	xìn rèn	trust; to trust; to have confidence in — 信 xìn (trustworthiness; to trust) + 任 rèn (to appoint; to entrust; to allow , 任 carries the sense of delegating responsibility to someone). To trust is to appoint a person as worthy of reliance. 信任危机 xìnrèn wéijī = a crisis of trust (institutional or personal). 建立信任 jiànlì xìnrèn = to build trust.
信息	xìn xī	information; news; data — 信 xìn (trustworthy word; communication) + 息 xī (breath; rest; news , 息 contains 自 zì, nose, over 心 xīn, heart: breath as the sign of life; by extension, news and tidings). A word that travels and carries meaning , information as a form of 信. 信息时代 xìnxī shídài = the information age.
信用	xìn yòng	creditworthiness; credit; trustworthiness in financial conduct — 信 xìn (trustworthiness) + 用 yòng (use; to use; practical application). Trustworthiness made actionable , the record of a person or institution reliably standing by its commitments. 信用卡 xìnyòng kǎ = credit card. 信用评级 xìnyòng píngjí = credit rating.
修改	xiū gǎi	to revise, to amend, to edit — 修 (mend, trim) + 改 (alter). To go back over a text, plan, or law and revise it. The standard word for editing a document or amending a draft.
羞耻心	xiū chǐ xīn	sense of shame
秀才	xiù cai	the lowest examination degree; a local scholar — The degree produced by the local examination (童试), the entry point of the examination ladder. A 秀才 had cleared the first hurdle and earned a recognized scholarly identity, but stood far from power.
虚实	xū shí	deficient and substantial
虚心	xū xīn	open-minded; receptive; humble in one's willingness to learn — 虚 xū (empty; hollow; receptive , the vessel with space in it) + 心 xīn (heart; the seat of thought and feeling in Chinese understanding). An empty heart , a mind that has not pre-filled itself with conclusions and so can genuinely receive what it encounters.
虚心学习	xū xīn xu é xí	to study with an open mind; to learn receptively — A fixed phrase in educational and developmental discourse. The combination of 虚心 (open-minded) + 学习 (to study; to learn) makes the stance toward learning explicit: approach the subject without assuming you already know, without defending existing positions, with the mind empty enough to receive new…
许可	xǔ kě	permission (formal noun)
悬河	xuán hé	Suspended river , a river whose bed has been raised by sediment deposition above the level of the surrounding plain, contained only by embankments. The lower Yellow River is the world's most extreme example, running up to 10 meters above the adjacent farmland.
学业	xu é yè	studies; schoolwork; academic work — 学 xué (to learn) + 业 yè (course of work). One's studies taken as a whole: 完成学业 "to complete one's studies," 学业成绩 "academic results." The general term for the body of work a student carries.
雪白	xu ě bái	snow-white; pure, dazzling white — 雪 xuě (snow) + 白 bái (white). The brightest, purest white, with snow as the standard of comparison, exactly parallel to English "snow-white." This is a productive pattern in Chinese: a vivid object plus a color word yields an intensified shade.
雪耻	xu ě chǐ	to wipe out a humiliation
雪花	xu ě huā	snowflake — 雪 xuě (snow) + 花 huā (flower). A snowflake, the snow seen as a falling blossom. The flower metaphor is old and pervasive in Chinese: snow falling is 雪花飘 (snow-flowers drifting), and the six-petaled structure of a real snow crystal was noticed early, with classical texts remarking that snow flowers h…
雪人	xu ěr én	snowman
雪域	xu ě yù	Snow Land — Snow Land: a poetic name for Tibet, used in both Chinese and Tibetan contexts. Refers to the snowcapped mountains and high plateau character of the Tibetan landscape and appears in poetry, song, and political rhetoric from multiple perspectives.
训练	xùn liàn	to train; training — 训 (to instruct, to discipline) + 练 (to drill). Organized, directed training: athletes, soldiers, dogs, new staff. Heavier and more institutional than 练习.
压力	yā lì	pressure; stress (physical or mental) — 压 yā (to press down) + 力 lì (force). The force pressing from outside. In physics it is pressure on a surface; in daily life it is the weight of stress: 工作压力 "work stress," 压力很大 "under a lot of pressure."
研究方向	yán jiū fāng xiàng	research direction; field of specialization — The standard academic term for one's research focus. Appears on every Chinese CV, every graduate-school admission form, and every department web page. 你的研究方向是什么? — "What's your research direction?" — is the second question Chinese academics ask each other after the name.
颜色	yán sè	color (general term) — 颜 yán (color; face , the face radical) + 色 sè. The standard compound for "color." 什么颜色？"What color?" Also used figuratively: 给你点颜色看看 gěi nǐ diǎn yánsè kànkan = "I'll show you what I'm capable of" (lit. "let you see some color" , a threat or challenge to someone who has been underestimating you).
颜色象征	yán sè xiàng zhēng	Color Symbolism — 红 hóng (red) , the color of luck, celebration, life force. Worn at weddings, hung at new year, placed in red envelopes (红包 hóngbāo). 白 bái (white) , the color of mourning; worn at funerals, avoided at celebrations.
颜真卿	Yán Zhēn qīng	Yan Zhenqing — Tang 楷书 master
眼光	yǎng uāng	vision; taste; judgment; the discerning eye — 眼 yǎn + 光 guāng (light). Literally eye-light. The kind of seeing a person has , their taste in art, their judgment in business, their ability to spot a good investment or a good partner. 有眼光 yǒu yǎnguāng "to have a good eye"; 没眼光 "to have poor judgment"; 长远的眼光 "long-term vision."
眼花	yǎn huā	to have blurred vision; to be dazzled — 眼 yǎn (eye) + 花 huā (blurred). Eyes swimming or dazzled, unable to focus, from too much to look at or from dizziness: 看得我眼花 "it left my eyes swimming." 头晕眼花 is "dizzy and bleary."
眼界	yǎn jiè	horizon (of experience); breadth of one's outlook — 眼 yǎn + 界 jiè (boundary; realm). The boundary of what one's eye has taken in , how widely one has seen, traveled, read, lived. 开阔眼界 kāikuò yǎnjiè "to broaden one's horizons" is one of the standard reasons given in Chinese for travel, study abroad, and reading widely.
眼睛	yǎn jing	eye(s); the eyes as organ — 眼 yǎn + 睛 jīng (eyeball; pupil). The standard everyday word for "eyes" in spoken Chinese. Single 眼 alone almost never serves this role; for "her eyes are beautiful" you say 她的眼睛很美 not 她的眼很美. The 睛 is light and unstressed in pronunciation.
眼泪	yǎn lèi	tears — 眼 yǎn + 泪 lèi (tear). Standard word for tears. 流眼泪 liú yǎnlèi "to shed tears"; 擦眼泪 cā yǎnlèi "to wipe away tears." The classical/literary equivalent is just 泪 alone or 泪水 lèishuǐ.
眼力	yǎn lì	perceptiveness; discerning power; visual acuity — 眼 yǎn + 力 lì (strength). The strength of one's seeing , both literally (sharpness of vision) and figuratively (judgment, ability to spot quality or fakery). The skilled antique-dealer is praised for 眼力 in detecting forgeries.
眼皮	yǎn pí	eyelid — 眼 yǎn + 皮 pí (skin). Eyelid. The colloquial idiom 眼皮底下 yǎnpí dǐxià "right under one's eyelids" means "right in front of one, in plain sight" , usually used to describe something missed or stolen despite being right there.
阳光	yáng guāng	sunlight; sunshine; (of a person) sunny, positive, and open — 阳 yáng (yang; sun) + 光 guāng (light; radiance). Literal sunlight, and by extension a personality quality: someone who is 阳光 is warm, open, optimistic , they bring light into the room. The metaphor is exact: they embody yang qualities in their social presence.
阳明洞	Yáng míng dòng	Yangming Cave; the retreat for which he was named
阳台	yáng tái	balcony; terrace — 阳 yáng (sun) + 台 tái (platform; stage). The sun-platform: the balcony or open terrace of a home, valued precisely because it catches the light and is where laundry is hung to dry.
阳虚	yáng xū	yang deficiency — the yang warmth is insufficient; cold dominates — Classic 阳虚 symptoms: aversion to cold (怕冷 pà lěng), cold hands and feet, pale complexion, fatigue and low energy, frequent clear urination, low libido, loose stools.
阳宅	yáng zhái	yang dwelling = home for the living
养老	yǎng lǎo	elder care
摇头	yáo tóu	to shake the head
遥远	yáo yuǎn	remote; very far away; vast distance — 遥 yáo (far off; distant; also a walk-movement compound) + 远 yuǎn (far). Both components carry the meaning of distance, making 遥远 the intensified form: not just far, but vertiginously far. Used for cosmic distances, ancient history, or inaccessible places. Primarily literary and written.
一笔钱	yī bǐ qián	a sum of money — 一 yī (one) + 笔 bǐ (measure word) + 钱 qián (money). A definite, lump amount of money, the kind written down as a single entry: 一大笔钱 "a large sum," 这笔钱 "this sum of money."
一笔生意	yī bǐ shēng yì	a (single) business deal — 一 yī (one) + 笔 bǐ (measure word) + 生意 shēngyì (business). One transaction or deal, counted as a single recorded piece of business: 做一笔生意 "to do a deal," 一笔大生意 "a big deal."
一部电影	yí bù diàn yǐng	a film; one movie — 一 yī (one) + 部 bù (measure word) + 电影 diànyǐng (film). The standard way to count a film, treating it as a single complete work. The same 部 counts a 小说 (novel) or a 电视剧 (TV series).
一部手机	yí bù shǒu jī	a mobile phone; one cellphone — 一 yī (one) + 部 bù (measure word) + 手机 shǒujī (phone). 部 counts a phone as a self-contained device. You will also hear 一个手机 in casual speech, but 一部手机 is the proper classifier.
一部小说	yí bù xiǎo shuō	a novel; one work of fiction — 一 yī (one) + 部 bù (measure word) + 小说 xiǎoshuō (novel). 部 counts a full-length novel as one finished work. For a short story you would more likely use 篇 piān, the classifier for shorter writings.
一带一路	yī dài yī lù	Belt and Road Initiative
一到就	yī dào jiù	as soon as [someone/something] arrives — Productive frame for arrival-triggered actions. 他一到就开始工作 (He started working as soon as he arrived). Pairs naturally with 回来 huí lái (return) and 到家 dào jiā (get home): 一回来就 / 一到家就.
一点儿 · 部分	yī diǎn r bù fen	a little (mass) · a part (of a whole) — 一点儿 measures out a small amount of an uncountable substance: 一点儿水 (a little water), 会一点儿中文 (speaks a little Chinese). It pairs with mass nouns where 几 pairs with countables. 部分 bùfen slices a defined whole: 一部分学生 (a portion of the students), 大部分时间 (most of the time). The trap of 一点儿 vs.
一定要	yí dìng yào	must; be sure to; definitely will — The emphatic resolve-and-urging frame. For yourself it is determination: 我一定要赢 ("I'm determined to win"). For others it is earnest pressing: 你一定要照顾好自己 ("you really must take good care of yourself").
一队	yí duì	a column / band of (measure word) — 队 as a measure word counts people or animals moving in a formed line: a column of soldiers, a string of camels, a file of schoolchildren.
一份工作	yí fèn gōng zuò	a job (one position) — When 工作 means a discrete job or position, it is counted with the measure word 份 fèn, the same word used for a portion or a document. 一份工作 ("one job"), 两份工作 ("two jobs," as in working two jobs). When 工作 means work as an activity or workload, it takes no measure word: 工作很多 ("there's a lot of work").
一封信	yì fēng xìn	one letter — 封 also works as the measure word for letters and other sealed correspondence: each sealed packet counts as one 封. 一封信 is one letter; 一封电子邮件 (yì fēng diànzǐ yóujiàn), one email.
一国两制	yī guó liǎng zhì	"One country, two systems" , the constitutional principle under which Hong Kong was reunified with China in 1997. Proposed by Deng Xiaoping, it promised Hong Kong fifty years of maintaining its existing legal, economic, and social systems under Chinese sovereignty. The principle expires in 2047.
一件事	yī jiàn shì	a matter: one affair; one thing to deal with — 一 yī (one) + 件 jiàn (measure word) + 事 shì (matter; affair). 件 is the standard classifier for an affair, an errand, or a piece of business, anything that can be treated as a single, separable concern. You do not say 一个事 in careful speech; the matter takes 件.
一件衣服	yī jiàn yī fu	an item of clothing: one garment — 一 yī (one) + 件 jiàn (measure word) + 衣服 yīfu (clothing). 件 counts upper-body and one-piece garments: shirts, coats, dresses, sweaters. Lower-body garments take 条 tiáo instead (一条裤子 a pair of trousers, 一条裙子 a skirt), so the measure word itself signals which part of the body is meant.
一节课	yì jié kè	one class period; one lesson — 一 yī (one) + 节 jié (measure word) + 课 kè (class). 节 here is the counter for class periods, each a fixed segment of the school day. 上一节课 "to attend a class period," 两节课 "two periods."
一句话	yī jù huà	one remark; a single sentence; in a word — 一 yī (one) + 句 jù (measure word) + 话 huà (speech). A single spoken remark, counted with 句. Used adverbially, 一句话 means "in a word, to put it briefly."
一看就懂	yī kàn jiù dǒng	understand at a glance; self-explanatory — The prototype 一…就 sentence. Used to describe something immediately clear, a design that needs no instructions, or a person who grasps something with no effort. 这个图表一看就懂 (This chart is self-explanatory).
一路平安	yí lù píng ān	"safe journey": the standard farewell to a traveler — 一 yī (one, whole) + 路 lù (road) + 平安 píng'ān (peace, safety). "May the whole road be peaceful." The phrase you say at the airport, the train station, or as the car pulls away. Slightly more formal than 一路顺风 yílù shùnfēng ("may the wind be with you").
一头牛	yī tóu niú	one cow; a head of cattle — 一 yī (one) + 头 tóu (measure word) + 牛 niú (cow). Here 头 keeps its full tone as the classifier for large livestock: cattle, pigs, sheep, elephants. You count beasts by the head.
一向	yī xiàng	all along; consistently; up to now — 一 yī (one; throughout) + 向 xiàng (facing back along). A habit or state held unbroken up to the present: 他一向如此 "he has always been like this." Marks long-standing consistency, not a one-off.
依然	yī rán	still (literary, often emotional)
已故	yǐ gù	the late; deceased
以便	yǐ biàn	in order to facilitate; so as to make convenient; so that — 以 (in order to) + 便 (convenient; to facilitate). Introduces a purpose clause describing how the preceding action creates favorable conditions for what follows. Register: formal written Chinese.
以防	yǐ fáng	as a precaution against; in order to guard against — 以 (in order to) + 防 (to guard against; to defend). Often extended to 以防万一 yǐfáng wànyī , "in order to guard against the one-in-ten-thousand chance," i.e., as a just-in-case precaution. One of the most common 以 compounds in everyday written Chinese.
以后	yǐ hòu	after; afterward; in the future; from now on — 以 (positional marker) + 后 (behind; after). The paired counterpart to 以前. Stands alone for "in the future" or follows a time reference for "after X." Compare: 之后 zhīhòu (slightly more classical feel; often interchangeable) and 后来 hòulái (what happened next in a narrative , retrospective only).
以免	yǐ miǎn	in order to avoid; so as to prevent; lest — 以 (in order to) + 免 (to avoid; to exempt from). Introduces a clause describing what the preceding action is intended to prevent. Appears in instructions, warnings, and formal recommendations.
以前	yǐ qián	before; previously; in the past; formerly — 以 yǐ (by means of; from; at) + 前 qián (front; before). "From the front / from before": the time that stands in front of the present moment. The most versatile and common temporal use of 前. Used after a specific time phrase (三年以前, three years ago) or alone (以前, in the past, formerly).
以前 · 现在 · 以后	yǐ qián xiàn zài yǐ hòu	before · now · after: the relative frame — 以前 and 以后 attach to any reference point: 三年以前 (three years ago), 吃饭以后 (after eating), or stand alone to mean "formerly" and "from now on". 的时候 de shíhou (when …) frames simultaneity: 我小的时候 (when I was little).
以上	yǐ shàng	above (a number); the above-mentioned; and more — 以 yǐ (from; by means of; at) + 上 shàng (above; higher). Used after a number or category to mean "and above" or "the following." Three uses: quantitative (三十以上 = thirty or more), sequential (如以上所述 = as stated above), and categorical (十八岁以上 = eighteen and older).
以上 · 以下	yǐ shàng yǐ xià	above / the above-mentioned · below / the following · more than / less than — 以上 yǐshàng (以 + 上 above): covers both spatial above and "more than a quantity" (十八岁以上 , 18 and above) and "what has been stated above" in written discourse (以上是本次报告的主要内容 , the above is the main content of this report). 以下 yǐxià mirrors it exactly in the opposite direction.
以为	yǐ wéi	to (mistakenly) think; to assume incorrectly — 以 (taking) + 为 (to be; to regard as). In classical Chinese the construction meant "to regard X as Y." In modern speech, 以为 has narrowed to almost always imply that the assumption turned out to be wrong , making it a useful signal of correction or disappointment.
以我为中心	yǐ wǒ wéi zhōng xīn	self-centered; to place oneself at the center
以下	yǐ xià	below (a number); the following; under — 以 yǐ (from; from this point) + 下 xià (below; the following). Three uses mirror exactly those of its counterpart 以上: quantitative (十八岁以下 = under eighteen), sequential (以下是通知 = the following is an announcement), and organizational. The pair 以上/以下 frames all formal Chinese enumeration.
以中文版为准	yǐ Zhōng wén bǎn wéi zhǔn	the Chinese version governs — A bilingual contract needs a clause saying which version prevails if they conflict (如有冲突 rú yǒu chōngtū). Chinese courts and arbitrators work in Chinese, so the Chinese text is the one that will be read. Have it translated back to you independently rather than trusting that the two versions match.
议论	yì lùn	to comment on; to talk about (often behind the scenes) — 议 yì (to deliberate, to propose) + 论. To comment on or talk about something, frequently with an undertone of gossip or unsolicited opinion. 议论纷纷 yìlùn fēnfēn describes a crowd buzzing with opinions. Also the name of a school essay genre: 议论文 yìlùnwén, the argumentative essay.
易经	Yì jīng	Book of Changes (a Five Classic)
意见	yì jiàn	opinion; view; objection — 意 yì (notion) + 见 jiàn (to see; view). What the mind sees on a matter, that is, a considered opinion: 提意见 "to offer a view." It can lean negative, where 有意见 means to have a complaint or grievance.
意境	yì jìng	the poetic realm — atmosphere, mood, and implied space — 意 yì (meaning; intention; feeling) + 境 jìng (realm; situation; space). The characteristic Chinese aesthetic concept of the space created beyond the words themselves: what the poem evokes rather than describes. Good poetry has 意境; pedestrian verse merely reports facts.
意识	yì shí	consciousness; awareness
意外	yì wài	unexpected; an accident; a mishap — 意 yì (notion; expectation) + 外 wài (outside). Outside what the heart anticipated, hence unexpected: 意外的消息 "surprising news." As a noun it names an accident or mishap that fell beyond anyone's reckoning.
意象	yì xiàng	Image, imagistic motif — Image, imagistic motif. Tang criticism valued the evocative image (意象) over explicit statement , show, don't tell, carried to its highest refinement.
意在笔先	yì zài bǐ xiān	The intention precedes the brush — The intention precedes the brush: the principle that a painter must have fully conceived the work before beginning to paint, because ink on xuan paper cannot be corrected. Applies equally to calligraphy.
因此	yīn cǐ	"Therefore" or "for this reason — "Therefore" or "for this reason." More formal than 所以, common in written and considered spoken contexts. Often used to introduce a conclusion drawn from preceding points: 因此, 我们决定… ("therefore, we decided…").
因而	yīn ér	thus; as a result; consequently — 因 yīn (because of) + 而 ér (and so). A written connective that draws a result from a stated cause, close to 因此 but smoother in flowing prose: 准备充分，因而很顺利 "well prepared, and so it went smoothly."
因果	yīng uǒ	cause and effect; (Buddhism) karma — 因 yīn (cause) + 果 guǒ (fruit; result). The cause-and-effect pair as a single concept, from the everyday 因果关系 "causal relationship" to the Buddhist law of karma, where every act ripens into its fruit.
因素	yīn sù	factor; element (contributing to a result) — 因 yīn (cause) + 素 sù (element). One of the contributing causes that feed into an outcome, used of the many partial causes behind a complex result: 经济因素 "economic factors," 不安全因素 "safety hazards."
因为	yīn wèi	because; due to — 因 yīn (cause; reason; because of) + 为 wèi (for; on account of). The most important conjunction in the language for expressing cause. Pairs with 所以 suǒyǐ (therefore) to form the standard causal construction: 因为 X，所以 Y (because X, therefore Y).
音乐会	yīn yu è huì	concert — Music + 会 (gathering), usually implying the classical hall; a pop star's arena show is a 演唱会 (yǎnchànghuì, "performed-singing gathering"). Tickets for either are 票, and 抢票 ("snatching tickets") describes the purchase experience accurately.
音乐家	yīn yu è jiā	musician (mastery level); composer — 音乐 yīnyuè (music) + 家 jiā. Implies serious, dedicated musical mastery: a composer, a concert performer, a conductor rather than a casual musician. Distinguished from 歌手 gēshǒu (singer/pop artist) by the 家 suffix, which implies depth, discipline, and classical standing.
音量	yīn liàng	volume; sound level
音信	yīn xìn	news; word; tidings (of a person) — 音 yīn (sound; word) + 信 xìn (message; letter). News or word of someone far away. Most common in the negative: 没有音信 "no word at all," and the idiom 杳无音信, vanished without a trace.
音韵	yīn yùn	Guanyin rhyme — Tieguanyin's signature quality
银河	yín hé	the Milky Way — 银 (yín, silver) + 河 (river). A silver river across the night sky. Chinese named the galaxy as a river of stars long before it was a galaxy, and the metaphor stuck.
应当	yīng dāng	ought to; should; be obliged to — 应 yīng (should) + 当 dāng (be fitting). A modal of obligation, near-identical in meaning to 应该 yīnggāi but slightly more formal, so it shows up often in rules, contracts, and writing.
应付	yìng fu	to cope with; to handle; to deal with — 应 yìng (to meet) + 付 fù (to hand over, to deal out). To handle a task or person, often a difficult one. Can carry a tinge of just getting through it: 应付考试 (manage to get through the exam), 应付了事 (do something perfunctorily just to be done with it).
应该是	yīng gāi shì	should be; probably is
应聘	yìng pìn	to apply for a job
应用	yìng yòng	to apply; application
英明	yīng míng	brilliant; enlightened (of a leader) — 英 yīng (outstanding; heroic; talent that flowers) + 明 míng (luminous). The compound used to praise rulers, commanders, and decision-makers of exceptional vision. 英明的领导 "an enlightened leader."
迎接	yíng jiē	to welcome, to go out to meet — 迎 (yíng, to go toward) + 接 (to receive). You walk out to meet an arrival and bring them in. Often warm or ceremonial: greeting guests, soldiers, the new year.
硬件	yìng jiàn	hardware
永乐帝	Yǒng lè Dì	third Ming emperor, builder of Beijing
永明延寿	Yǒng míng Yán shòu	Yongming Yanshou (904–975 CE) — Song dynasty Chan-Pure Land synthesis — The sixth patriarch of Pure Land and a major Chan master simultaneously, Yanshou embodied the Chinese Buddhist principle that the two traditions are not opposed but complementary.
用法	yòng fǎ	usage; how something is used — 用 yòng (to use) + 法 fǎ (way). The way a thing is properly used, especially a word or a tool: 这个词的用法 "the usage of this word." Built on the same "way of doing" sense as 方法.
用力	yòng lì	to exert force; to use one's strength; hard (of physical effort) — 用 yòng (to use) + 力 lì (force). To put physical force into an action: 用力推 "push hard," 用力拉 "pull with all your strength." Describes how an action is done, with muscle behind it.
用途	yòng tú	use; purpose; application; intended function — 用 yòng (use; function) + 途 tú (route; destination; intended course). The destination of deployment: what something is for, where its use is headed. 用途 answers the question "what is this for?" and tends to describe a range of applications rather than a single act of using.
用心	yòng xīn	attentively; with care
优美	yōu měi	graceful; elegant
由于	yóu yú	due to; owing to; because of — 由 yóu (from) + 于 yú (at; in). A formal, written near-synonym of 因为, marking the cause at the front of a sentence: 由于天气原因，航班取消了 "owing to the weather, the flight was cancelled." It pairs with 因此 rather than 所以.
犹如	yóu rú	just as; like
邮件	yóu jiàn	mail; email; a postal item — 邮 yóu (post; mail) + 件 jiàn (a piece). Originally any item sent through the post. In everyday modern use it most often means email: 电子邮件 diànzǐ yóujiàn (electronic mail) is regularly shortened to 邮件. To send one is 发邮件 fā yóujiàn.
油画	yóu huà	oil painting — 油 yóu (oil) + 画 huà (painting). The Western art of painting in oils, named for its medium. Its native counterpart is 国画 guóhuà, named for its nation rather than its material.
游船	yóu chuán	pleasure boat, cruise boat
友情	yǒu qíng	friendship; the feeling between friends — 友 yǒu (friend) + 情 qíng (feeling). The bond of friendship. Close to 友谊 yǒuyì in meaning, but 友情 leans on the felt warmth, while 友谊 leans on the formal relationship.
有为	yǒu wéi	capable; purposeful; with ambition and scope to act; achieving — 有 yǒu (to have; to possess) + 为 wéi (action; doing; purposeful agency). The counterpart to 无为 , intentional, directed, deliberate action.
有问题	yǒu wèn tí	there's a problem; there's a question — The genuinely ambiguous phrase: it can mean "I have a question" or "there's a problem / something's wrong," and only context disambiguates. 有问题吗？after an explanation means "any questions?"; 这里有问题 about a machine means "there's a problem here."
有用	yǒu yòng	useful; of use
又红又专	yòu hóng yòu zhuān	both red and expert; politically loyal and professionally capable — 又 yòu...又 yòu (both...and) framing 红 (red, politically reliable) and 专 zhuān (expert, specialized). A defining slogan of the Mao era: the ideal cadre or worker was supposed to be ideologically loyal and technically skilled at once.
右边	yòu biān	the right side; on the right — 右 (right) + 边 (side; edge). The neutral everyday word for "the right side." 在右边 (on the right), 右边的窗户 (the window on the right). The paired form 左边 zuǒbiān (the left side) appears constantly alongside it.
右派	yòu pài	the right faction; right-wing — 右 (right) + 派 (faction, school, stream). Modern political term adopted from Western usage. In PRC political history, 右派 took on specific weight during the Anti-Rightist Campaign (反右运动 Fǎn Yòu Yùndòng, 1957–1959) launched after the Hundred Flowers Campaign.
右手	yòu shǒu	the right hand — 右 (right) + 手 (hand). The right hand , the dominant hand in the original character, the hand raised to the mouth. In Chinese calligraphy instruction, 右手执笔 (hold the brush with the right hand) was the standard prescription.
于是	yú shì	thereupon; as a result; and so — 于 yú (at; in; given) + 是 (this; the situation). Classical origin: "at this point; given this situation." A narrative conjunction that advances a story , signaling consequence or the next development in a sequence. Slightly formal register.
盂兰盆节	Yú lán pén Jié	Ghost Festival — the great Buddhist-folk religion synthesis — Held on the 15th day of the 7th lunar month, the midpoint of the "Ghost Month" 鬼月 during which the spirits of the dead are believed to return to the world of the living. Families set out food offerings, burn paper goods, and float river lanterns to guide the spirits.
娱乐	yú lè	entertainment; recreation; to amuse oneself — 娱 yú (to amuse) + 乐 lè (joy). Entertainment or recreation, both the activities and the having of fun: 娱乐活动 "recreational activities," 娱乐圈 "the entertainment world."
渔船	yú chuán	fishing boat — 渔 (to fish) + 船 (boat): a boat worked for the catch. 渔 carries the water radical 氵, marking the act of fishing, distinct from 鱼 the fish itself.
愉快	yú kuài	pleasant; cheerful; agreeable — 愉 yú (pleased; at ease; the heart-radical with a sound component suggesting relaxed breathing) + 快 kuài (unobstructed joy). More refined and slightly more formal than 快乐. The word you reach for when describing an experience, a meeting, or an atmosphere as genuinely pleasant.
舆论	yú lùn	public opinion — 舆 yú (the common people; literally a carriage, by extension the public) + 论. The collective voice of the public on an issue: public opinion as a force. 舆论压力 yúlùn yālì "pressure of public opinion," 引导舆论 yǐndǎo yúlùn "to guide public opinion."
雨季	yǔ jì	rainy season
雨天	yǔ tiān	rainy day
语法	yǔ fǎ	grammar — 语 yǔ (language; speech) + 法 fǎ (rule; law). The "law of language," the system of rules by which a language is built. The same 法 of fixed standard, here governing how words combine.
语录	yǔ lù	recorded sayings of Chan masters
语文	yǔ wén	language arts; Chinese (as a school subject) — 语 yǔ (language; speech) + 文 wén (writing; culture; literature). The Chinese school subject covering reading, writing, classical texts, and composition , the equivalent of "English" class in an English-speaking curriculum, but broader in scope because it encompasses classical Chinese literature along…
语音	yǔ yīn	voice message — Factory staff often send long voice messages. 转文字 (zhuǎn wénzì) converts a voice message to text inside WeChat (press and hold the message), and the result is easy to paste into a translator. Asking for text is acceptable when phrased as your limitation.
预报	yù bào	forecast
预定	yù dìng	to book in advance; to reserve; to schedule — 预 yù (beforehand) + 定 dìng (to settle). To settle something ahead of time: a table, a room, a plan. Often written 预订 when the matter booked is goods or tickets. The 预 fixes the settling in the future.
寓意	yù yì	allegorical meaning; moral
元宵节	Yuán xiāo Jié	Lantern Festival
原来如此	yuán lái rú cǐ	"Oh, so that's how it is!" or "I see now — "Oh, so that's how it is!" or "I see now." The classic "aha" of comprehension. The pieces have just clicked together. Slightly literary in feel but used constantly in everyday speech. The looser 原来是这样 (yuánlái shì zhèyàng) is the more colloquial version.
原因	yuán yīn	cause; reason — 原 yuán (origin; source) + 因 yīn (cause). The standard noun for the cause or reason behind something: 失败的原因 "the reason for the failure." Its pair is 结果 jiéguǒ, the result.
原因分析	yuán yīn fēn xī	cause analysis — The first thing to ask for. 根本原因 (gēnběn yuányīn) is the root cause; an answer like 工人操作不当 (worker error) usually is not one. Ask why the process allowed the error.
原则上同意	yuán zé shang tóng yì	agree in principle — Signals agreement on direction while keeping the details open. Useful when the boss pushes for a handshake before the numbers are settled.
圆明园	Yuán míng yuán	Old Summer Palace (burned 1860)
缘故	yuáng ù	reason; cause; the why behind something — 缘 (yuán, cause, affinity, the edge along which things connect) + 故 (cause, reason). A two-character word for the reason behind an event, slightly more formal and reflective than the everyday 原因 (yuányīn).
源头	yuán tóu	Source, literally "source head — Source, literally "source head." The Yangtze's source was definitively surveyed only in the 1970s–80s, deep in the Tanggula Mountains on the Tibetan Plateau. The identification of a river's 源头 carries cosmological weight; knowing where a river begins is knowing something fundamental about the world.
远处	yuǎn chù	in the distance; a distant place — 远 yuǎn (far) + 处 chù (place; location). The place that is far away, viewed from where you stand. A positional noun used as a subject or object, always indicating a vague far-off point rather than a named destination.
远大	yuǎn dà	ambitious; far-reaching; grand (of goals or ideals) — 远 yuǎn (far) + 大 dà (great; large). Applied to goals, plans, and ideals that extend far into the future or require enormous scope to realize.
远方	yuǎn fāng	a distant place; far-off land; the faraway — 远 yuǎn (far) + 方 fāng (direction; region; place). More evocative than 远处: 远方 carries a tone of longing or aspiration. Where 远处 names a physical spot in view, 远方 names a horizon you are drawn toward. Common in poetry, lyrics, and travel writing.
远见	yuǎn jiàn	foresight, long-range vision
远离	yuǎn lí	to keep far from; to stay away from; to leave behind — 远 yuǎn (far) + 离 lí (to leave; to separate from). A deliberate act of putting distance between yourself and something: a place, a person, a habit. Carries volition. Used in health campaigns (远离烟酒, stay away from tobacco and alcohol), personal advice, and literature.
远亲	yuǎn qīn	distant relative; far-off kin — 远 yuǎn (distant) + 亲 qīn (kin; close; intimate). A relative at the outer edge of the family network.
远山	yuǎn shān	distant mountains
愿意	yuàn yì	to be willing; to want to — 愿 yuàn (to wish) + 意 yì (intent). The willingness of the heart to do something: 我愿意帮你 "I'm willing to help you." It expresses consent and readiness, not mere ability.
月老	Yu è lǎo	the Old Man Under the Moon; deity of marriage and romantic fate — 月 yuè (moon) + 老 lǎo (old; elderly; venerable). Yuèlǎo appears at night under the moon, checking his celestial book of destined marriages, and tying the red threads that will unite couples across any distance. 拜月老 (to pray to Yuèlǎo) is a common practice among those seeking romantic fate.
粤语	Yu è yǔ	Cantonese
越发	yu è fā	increasingly (literary)
越过	yu è guò	to cross over; to get past — 越 + 过 (guò, to pass). The most basic verb of crossing: a fence, a stream, a hurdle, a difficulty. Highly productive in both literal and figurative senses.
越界	yu è jiè	to cross a boundary; to overstep
越南	Yu èn án	Vietnam (lit. "south of Yue")
越权	yu è quán	to overstep one's authority — 越 + 权 (quán, authority; power). Acting beyond what your position permits , a serious accusation in any organization, especially in the bureaucratic hierarchies that have shaped Chinese institutional life since imperial times.
运河	yùn hé	canal — 运 (yùn, to transport / haul) + 河 (river). A dug waterway built to carry boats and freight, a river made by labor rather than rain. The Grand Canal is 大运河 (dà yùnhé).
运用	yùn yòng	to apply; to put into practice; to exercise (a skill) — 运 yùn (to move; to carry; to transport; to operate) + 用 yòng (to use). To bring knowledge or skill into active operation: using what one knows in a real context. Where 使用 is about deploying a tool, 运用 is about skillfully exercising something more abstract , a theory, a method, a body of knowledge.
杂草	zá cǎo	weeds — 杂 zá (mixed; miscellaneous) + 草 cǎo (grass). The unwanted, untended grass that crowds a garden or field, the plants you pull out rather than plant. 拔杂草 "to pull weeds."
宰相	zǎi xiàng	prime minister (of imperial times); chief minister — 宰 zǎi (to govern; to manage) + 相 xiàng (minister). The highest official under the emperor, charged with overseeing the government. A staple figure of historical drama and idiom.
再次	zài cì	once again; once more (formal)
再见	zài jiàn	goodbye (see you again) — The forward-looking again fused to the meet verb. Internet speech numbers it 88 (bābā, "bye-bye"), and the dramatic register upgrades to 永别 for farewells meant to be final.
再三	zài sān	again and again; repeatedly; over and over — 再 zài (again; once more) + 三 sān (three). Three times and then some: the most natural adverb of repeated action in Mandarin. Appears before a verb to signal that someone did something multiple times, often with increasing emphasis or urgency.
再说	zài shuō	we'll talk about it later; let's deal with it when the time comes; moreover (discourse marker) — 再 (later; further) + 说 (to speak; to deal with). Multiple uses: (1) Deferral: 这件事以后再说 (We'll deal with this later , let's not now). Very common for postponing a conversation or decision. (2) Discourse marker meaning "moreover; besides": 再说，我也没空 (Besides, I'm not free either).
再也	zài yě	never again (with negation)
再者	zài zhě	furthermore; in addition; moreover (formal transition) — 再 (again; further; additionally) + 者. A discourse connector used in formal writing and spoken argument to introduce an additional point: "and there is also this." More formal than 另外 lìngwài (besides) and slightly more deliberate in pacing than 而且 érqiě (moreover).
在于	zài yú	to lie in; to rest with; to consist in — 在 zài (to be at) + 于 yú (at). Points to where the heart of a matter resides: 问题在于 "the problem lies in," 关键在于 "the key rests with." A staple of explanatory and argumentative prose.
造句	zào jù	to make a sentence; to compose a sentence — 造 zào (to make; to build) + 句 jù (sentence). The classroom exercise of building a sentence to practice a new word, a verb-object compound: 用 "因为" 造句 "make a sentence with 'because'."
噪音	zào yīn	noise — 噪 (clamor, wearing three mouths) + 音. The urban complaint word: construction 噪音, traffic 噪音, and the neighbor's renovation drill. 噪音污染 (noise pollution) is a recognized legal complaint category in Chinese cities.
怎么 + 动词	zěn me + ve r b	how (to do something); in what way — The core use: placed before a verb, 怎么 asks about the method or manner of an action. 怎么走 ("how to get there"), 怎么说 ("how to say it"), 怎么用 ("how to use it").
战争前的仪式	zhàn zhēng qián de yí shì	pre-battle ritual — the shaman as military consultant — Before major military campaigns, Tungusic and Mongolian rulers consulted their shamans , divining the auspiciousness of the timing, the direction of attack, and the likely outcome.
章节	zhāng jié	chapters and sections (of a book) — 章 zhāng (chapter) + 节 jié (section). The structural divisions of a written work taken together. A book's text is segmented into 章 and 节 the way a stalk is segmented by its knots.
长短	cháng duǎn	length; the long and short of it — 长 (cháng, long) bound to 短 (duǎn, short): a paired opposite naming the dimension of length itself. Figuratively it means the rights and wrongs, or any unforeseen mishap.
长期合作	cháng qī hé zuò	long-term cooperation — The phrase that justifies concessions on both sides: the factory gives on price for the sake of future orders, you give on terms for the sake of a stable partner. Invoke it when asking for something; hear it as a request when the other side uses it.
找到	zhǎo dào	to find; to locate — 找 zhǎo (to search for; to look for) + 到 dào (arrive). Searching that arrived at its target: the thing was found. 找 alone is the act of searching; 找到 is the confirmed discovery. 找不到 (can't find it) and 找到了 (found it!) are extremely high-frequency in daily speech.
找工作	zhǎo gōng zuò	to look for a job; to job-hunt — The set phrase for job-hunting. 找 (to look for) plus 工作. Common in life and conversation, especially among graduates and people changing roles: 毕业以后我要找工作 ("after I graduate I'll look for a job"). The result of a successful search is 找到工作 ("found a job"), with 到 marking the achieved result.
赵州狗子	Zhào zhōu gǒu zi	Zhaozhou's dog — the gateway kōan of Chinese Chan — Master Zhaozhou Congshen 赵州从谂 (778–897 CE) was asked: "Does a dog have Buddha nature?" He replied: 无 wú. Since Buddhist doctrine holds that all sentient beings have Buddha nature, "no" is doctrinally wrong , but so is "yes" if it's merely conceptual.
照明	zhào míng	illumination; lighting (noun &amp; verb) — 照 zhào (to shine upon; to illuminate) + 明 míng (bright). Used for artificial lighting systems, stage lighting, and the act of shining a light on something. 照明设备 = lighting equipment.
照相	zhào xiàng	to take a photograph — 照 zhào (to shine; to reflect) + 相 xiàng (appearance). To fix a likeness in a picture: 给我照相 "take a photo of me." A separable verb: 照了一张相 "took one photo."
这就是为什么	zhè jiù shì wèi shén me	"That's why — "That's why." Used after explaining a cause to land the consequence with emphasis. 这就是为什么我不喜欢他 ("that's why I don't like him"). Slightly emphatic; signals you have just arrived at the punchline.
着急	zháo jí	to worry, to feel anxious — 着 (zháo, to be caught by) + 急 (urgency). To be seized by anxiety. This is the inward state of 急: a fretting heart, not a fast body. It is the most common everyday word for feeling worried over something time-sensitive.
着急上火	zháo jí shàng huǒ	frantic with worry
针眼	zhēn yǎn	the eye of a needle; (medical) sty — 针 zhēn (needle) + 眼. The hole in a sewing needle , the same metaphor English uses. Also a colloquial term for a sty on the eyelid (the small swelling looking like a tiny eye).
真诚	zhēn chéng	sincere, in earnest (in attitude or expression) — 真 zhēn + 诚 chéng (sincerity; the Confucian virtue of inner-outer alignment). Sincere in attitude: an apology, a wish, a smile, a thank-you that is not just formal courtesy but actually meant. The diplomatic and written register.
真的假的	zhēn de jiǎ de	"Seriously?" Literally "true or fake," used as an emphatic version of 真的吗 when something sounds surprising or hard to believe. Common among younger speakers and friends; slightly informal for use with elders or in professional settings.
真的吗	zhēn de ma	really? for real? — The most common follow-up question in casual conversation, expressing surprise. 真的 on its own answers it: "Yes, really." Often softened to 真假 (zhēn jiǎ, "real or fake?") in playful or informal speech.
真好	zhēn hǎo	really nice; truly good; that's lovely — The all-purpose appreciation. 真好! works as a stand-alone exclamation: "wonderful!" 真好的人 "a really good person." 这里真好 "this place is really nice."
真理	zhēn lǐ	truth (philosophical, capital-T) — 真 zhēn + 理 lǐ (principle; pattern; reason). The truth in the philosophical, abstract sense , the deep principle that governs, the ultimate reality. The word used in religious, scientific, and political discourse for capital-T Truth.
真实	zhēn shí	real, factual, actually existing — 真 zhēn + 实 shí (solid; real; substance). The factual register: actually-the-case, in contrast to fabricated, fictional, or virtual. Used for stories, events, identities, statistics.
真讨厌	zhēn tǎo yàn	how annoying; ugh — Light complaint, often spoken with a sigh. The 真 marks the speaker's genuine irritation rather than a measured judgment.
真相	zhēn xiàng	the truth; the real state of affairs — 真 zhēn (true) + 相 xiàng (appearance; face). The true face of a matter once the surface is stripped away: 事情的真相 "the truth of the matter," 揭露真相 "to expose the truth."
真心	zhēn xīn	a sincere heart; from the heart (adv.) — 真 zhēn + 心 xīn (heart). Sincerity located at the heart-level: not just attitude (真诚) but the felt inner orientation. As a noun: 一片真心 "a heart full of sincerity." As an adverb: 真心爱你 "I love you from the heart."
真正	zhēn zhèng	genuine, true (the real thing, not the imitation) — 真 zhēn + 正 zhèng (correct; proper; orthodox). The genuine-article register: this is the real one, the one that lives up to the name, not the imitation. Common attributively before nouns.
镇定	zhèn dìng	composed; calm; to keep one's cool — 镇 zhèn (to press down; to keep calm) + 定 dìng (settled). The steadiness of a person under pressure, a cool head when others panic: 保持镇定 "to stay composed," 镇定自若 "perfectly calm and self-possessed."
争论	zhēng lùn	to dispute; controversy
整理	zhěng lǐ	to tidy up; to sort; to put in order — 整 zhěng (whole; to straighten) + 理 lǐ (to put in order). To arrange scattered things back into order: 整理房间 "to tidy the room," 整理笔记 "to organize one's notes."
知己	zhī jǐ	a bosom friend; one who truly knows you — 知 zhī (to know) + 己 jǐ (self). One who knows your self, the rare friend who understands you completely. The famous line 人生得一知己足矣 holds that to gain a single true friend in life is enough.
知名	zhī míng	well-known; famous; noted — 知 zhī (to know; to be known) + 名 míng (name; fame; renown). Literally: one whose name is known. 知名 sits in the formal-written register; everyday speech prefers 有名 yǒumíng (has fame) or 出名 chūmíng (name has come out). 知名度 zhīmíngdù = degree of fame, public profile.
知青	zhī qīng	educated sent-down youth
知识	zhī shi	knowledge; information; factual learning — 知 zhī (to know; to understand) + 识 shí (to recognize; to discern; knowledge gained through recognition). Factual, storable, transmissible knowledge , what fills textbooks, databases, and examinations. 知识分子 zhīshi fènzǐ (intellectual; educated person , lit.
知音	zhī yīn	soulmate; the one who truly understands — From Boya and Ziqi: one who knows your tone. Weightier than 朋友 (friend); to call someone 知音 claims they understand what you mean beneath what you say. The lament 知音难觅 ("a zhiyin is hard to find") is the cultured way to say good friends are rare.
知者	zhī zhě	the one who knows; the wise person
直接	zhí jiē	direct, directly — 直 (zhí, straight) + 接 (join). The two ends meet with nothing in between, a straight join. As an adverb it means doing a thing with no go-between, no detour.
职业	zhí yè	occupation; profession; vocational — 职 zhí (post; duty) + 业 yè (work). The line of work a person practices for a living: 职业选择 "choice of profession." As a modifier it means professional or vocational: 职业运动员 "professional athlete," 职业学校 "vocational school."
植物	zhí wù	plant
只有才	zhǐ yǒu cái	only if…then (necessary condition)
指明方向	zhǐ míng fāng xiàng	to point out the direction; to provide guidance — 指 (zhǐ, to point) + 明 (míng, clear) + 方向. The fixed phrase for guidance from a teacher, leader, or authority. Common in educational and political registers , a teacher 为学生指明方向, a leader 为国家指明方向. Carries a slightly elevated, often exhortatory tone.
指南针	zhǐn án zhēn	compass (the navigational instrument) — 指 (to point) + 南 (south) + 针 (needle). A south-pointing needle: the Chinese compass was designed to point south rather than north, in keeping with the cosmological primacy of the south.
至于	zhì yú	as for
志向	zhì xiàng	aspiration; ambition; the goal one is set on — 志 zhì (will; resolve) + 向 xiàng (direction). The direction of one's will, what one aspires to: 远大的志向 "lofty aspirations." The facing of ambition, where a person has set their life.
质量	zhì liàng	quality: how good something is (also physical mass) — 质 zhì (substance, nature) + 量 liàng (measure). In everyday speech 质量 is quality, the standard or grade of a product or piece of work: 质量好 (good quality), 提高质量 (to raise quality). In physics the same word is the technical term for mass. Context separates the two cleanly.
中秋节	Zhōng qiū jié	Mid-Autumn Festival
中文系	zhōng wén xì	department of Chinese (at a university) — 中文 zhōngwén (Chinese language) + 系 xì (department). The Chinese department of a university. The bare 系 is the academic department itself, so any field plugs in front of it: 数学系 math department, 法律系 law department.
中心	zhōng xīn	center; central point; hub — 中 zhōng (middle) + 心 xīn (heart). Lit. "middle heart." The conceptual and physical center of something: a city, an argument, an organization. Used freely in both spatial and abstract senses. Extremely productive in compound formation: any institution can become a 中心 by adding its domain.
中元节	Zhōng yuán Jié	the Ghost Festival — Daoist name for the 15th of the 7th month — 中 zhōng (middle) + 元 yuán (origin; first; primary) + 节 jié (festival; season). The Daoist name, derived from the Three Officials calendar: 上元 (upper prime, 1st month, 15th), 中元 (middle prime, 7th month, 15th), 下元 (lower prime, 10th month, 15th). Each 元 is the birthday of one official.
重量	zhòng liàng	weight (the physical quantity) — 重 zhòng (heavy) + 量 liàng (quantity; measure). The amount of heaviness, weight as measured: 重量很轻 "the weight is light." The bodily version is 体重 tǐzhòng, one's body weight.
重心	zhòng xīn	center of gravity; focus; emphasis — 重 zhòng (heavy; weight) + 心 xīn (heart; center). A physics term that has crossed fully into metaphorical use: where the weight of attention, effort, or strategic priority lies. Used in business planning, policy discussions, and personal goal-setting.
重新	chóng xīn	anew; afresh; once more (from the start) — 重 chóng (again) + 新 xīn (new). To do something over from a fresh start: 重新开始 "to start anew," 重新考虑 "to reconsider." Stronger than a plain 又, it implies a clean slate.
重阳	Chóng yáng	Double Ninth; Double Yang — the festival name — 重 chóng (double; repeated; layered) + 阳 yáng (yang; the masculine creative principle; odd numbers; heaven). The name describes the cosmological condition of the date: two nine-yang numbers coinciding.
重阳糕	Chóng yáng gāo	Double Ninth cake — the city dweller substitution — 重阳 Chóngyáng (the festival) + 糕 gāo (cake; steamed or baked confection). The cleverest detail of the festival: 糕 (gāo, cake) is homophonous with 高 (gāo, high).
重阳节	Chóng yáng Jié	Double Ninth Festival (autumn)
周公	Zhōu gōng	Duke of Zhou
珠心算	zhū xīn suàn	Abacus mental arithmetic — Abacus mental arithmetic: performing suanpan calculations by visualizing the instrument in the mind. Neurologically distinct from verbal mental arithmetic: brain imaging shows practitioners use visuospatial rather than language regions.
主持	zhǔ chí	to host; to preside over; to chair — 主 zhǔ (to preside) + 持 chí (to hold). To run an event from the chair: to host a show, chair a meeting, or take charge of a project. The host of a broadcast is a 主持人 zhǔchírén.
主动	zhǔ dòng	to take the initiative; proactive; of one's own accord — 主 zhǔ (master; to take charge) + 动 dòng (to act). To move first and on one's own, rather than waiting to be pushed. Its opposite is 被动 bèidòng (passive). 主动帮忙 "to help without being asked."
主观	zhǔ guān	subjective
主权	zhǔ quán	sovereignty
主人	zhǔ rén	master; owner; host — 主 zhǔ (master) + 人 rén (person). The one who owns a thing or receives a guest: an animal's owner, a house's master, the host of a gathering. Its pair is 客人 kèrén, the guest.
主食	zhǔ shí	Staple food — Staple food: the rice, noodles, or dumplings served at the end of a Chinese meal. The late arrival of the staple signals that the banquet was generous enough that no one needed to fill up on carbohydrates.
主题	zhǔ tí	theme; central subject; motif — 主 zhǔ (main; principal) + 题 tí (theme). The central idea that organizes a work, an event, or a piece of music: 电影的主题 "the theme of the film," 主题公园 "a theme park." It is the heading that everything else serves.
主体	zhǔ tǐ	subject, agent
主位	zhǔ wèi	The seat of honor at a table — The seat of honor at a table: typically facing the door, with one's back to the kitchen or wall. Assigning the 主位 to the senior guest or the person being celebrated is a face-giving act.
主席	zhǔ xí	chairman; chairperson; president (of a body) — 主 zhǔ (presiding) + 席 xí (seat; mat). Literally "the presiding seat," the one who sits at the head of a meeting or a state. Used for the chair of a committee and for a head of state.
主要	zhǔ yào	main; primary; principal — 主 zhǔ (master; host; main; primary) + 要 (essential). "The main/essential one." Used to identify the principal element among several: 主要问题 (the main problem) · 主要原因 (the primary reason) · 主要目标 (the primary goal). Common in analysis, journalism, and academic writing.
主意	zhǔ yi	idea; plan; decision — 主 zhǔ (governing) + 意 yì (intention). The plan one settles on, the idea one acts by: 好主意 "a good idea," 拿主意 "to make up one's mind." Note the neutral tone on yi.
主张	zhǔ zhāng	to advocate; to maintain; a stance; a position — 主 zhǔ (to decide) + 张 zhāng (to stretch; to spread). As a verb, to put forward and stand by a view; as a noun, the view itself: 我主张和平 "I advocate peace," 这是我的主张 "this is my position."
助手	zhù shǒu	assistant; helper
注定	zhù dìng	destined; fated
注意	zhù yì	to pay attention; to take note; to watch out — 注 zhù (to pour; to fix) + 意 yì (notion; attention). To pour the mind onto something, that is, to pay attention: 注意安全 "mind your safety," 请注意 "please take note."
专业	zhuān yè	major; specialty; professional, specialized — 专 zhuān (to specialize; sole) + 业 yè (field of study). As a noun, the major one studies: 我的专业是历史 "my major is history." As an adjective, professional or specialized: 专业知识 "specialized knowledge," 很专业 "very professional."
转换	zhuǎn huàn	to switch, convert
转经筒	zhuǎn jīng tǒng	Prayer wheel — Prayer wheel: a cylindrical object containing mantras, rotated clockwise by practitioners. Each rotation is considered equivalent to reciting the mantra contained within.
壮美	zhuàng měi	sublime; grand beauty
状语	zhuàng yǔ	adverbial modifier
子女	zǐn ǚ	sons and daughters; children (collectively) — 子 zǐ (sons) + 女 nǚ (daughters). A formal, collective term for one's children as a group, common in writing about family and law. Here 子 keeps its full third tone.
字眼	zì yǎn	the key word; the word one fastens on — 字 zì (character; word) + 眼. The eye of the phrase , the single word that carries the weight, that you pivot a sentence on, that an opponent picks at. 抠字眼 kōu zìyǎn "to pick at the wording" (often pejoratively, like English "splitting hairs over phrasing").
自从	zì cóng	ever since; from the time that — 自 zì (from) + 从 cóng (from; since). A conjunction marking a starting point in the past, paired with a later clause: 自从他来了以后 "ever since he arrived." Used only of time already begun, never the future.
自大	zì dà	self-aggrandizing; thinking oneself greater than one is; conceited — 自 zì (self) + 大 dà (great; large; important). Consistently negative , the failure of accurate self-assessment in the direction of inflation. Where 骄傲 can sometimes be legitimate pride, 自大 always describes a distortion: the person who claims more importance than they possess.
自动	zì dòng	automatic; automatically; of one's own accord — 自 zì (self) + 动 dòng (to move). Moving by itself, without anyone working it: 自动门 "automatic door," 自动售货机 "vending machine." Also of people acting unprompted: 自动报名 "to sign up on one's own."
自己	zì jǐ	oneself; myself, yourself, himself (etc.); one's own — 自 zì (self) + 己 jǐ (oneself). The everyday reflexive word, flexing to whatever person the sentence needs: 我自己 "myself," 你自己 "yourself." It also marks possession, 自己的房间 "one's own room," and stresses doing a thing without help.
自己人	zì jǐ rén	one of us; an insider; our own people — 自己 zìjǐ (oneself) + 人 rén. Someone who belongs to your group: your team, your family, your circle of trust. The implied contrast is always 外人 wàirén (outsider). 都是自己人，不用客气 "We're all family here, no need for formality" is an expression of warmth that lowers the social register instantly.
自家	zì jiā	one's own family/people; one's own — 自 zì (self; one's own) + 家 jiā (family; home). Marks something as belonging to or originating from one's own household. Central to the Chinese social concept of insiders vs. outsiders: 自家人 (one of our own) vs. 外人 wàirén (an outsider).
自力	zì lì	self-power (Chan's path)
自满	zì mǎn	complacent; self-satisfied; smug; resting on one's laurels — 自 zì (self) + 满 mǎn (full; satisfied). The state of being full , too full to receive more, too satisfied to keep improving. The classical warning 满招损 is precisely directed at 自满.
自谦	zì qiān	self-deprecating; to understate oneself
自然而然	zì rán ér rán	naturally and inevitably; of its own accord
自然界	zì rán jiè	the natural world; the realm of nature
自私	zì sī	selfish
自我	zì wǒ	the self; the ego; self- (in compounds) — 自 zì (self) + 我 wǒ (I; me). The self as an inner concept, the "ego" of psychology and philosophy. As a prefix it forms self-directed actions: 自我介绍 "self-introduction," 自我批评 "self-criticism."
自我介绍	zì wǒ jiè shào	self-introduction
自信	zì xìn	self-confident; confidence in oneself — 自 zì (self) + 信 xìn (to trust; believe). To trust in oneself: 他很自信 "he is very self-assured." A close cousin is 自尊 zìzūn (self-respect), another of the many self- compounds built on 自.
自由	zì yóu	freedom; liberty; free (to act) — 自 zì (self) + 由 yóu (to follow; from). Literally "following from oneself," to act from one's own will rather than another's: 言论自由 "freedom of speech," 行动自由 "freedom of movement."
自知	zì zhī	self-knowledge
自尊	zì zūn	self-esteem
